# The Authoring API Implementation Plan

> **WRITTEN 2026-09-25 — BEING EXECUTED. ITS TEN-SITTING SPLIT WAS APPROVED BY RICH ON 2026-09-25**, at the opening of sitting 1 (*How this plan is to be executed* has his four answers).
> Which sitting is next is stated by the sittings table below (and ORIENTATION §7e), and nowhere else. Every fact in
> *Read this first* was read from the code at `8534e2e` or measured on this machine while the plan was written; Task 1
> re-measures each one into `spikes/authoring-baseline/`. *What executing this plan found* is where every sitting's
> record goes.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. **Commit on `main`; no branch, no worktree, no push** — ORIENTATION §6 rule 9, which both of those skills will push you against.

**Goal:** An application can be CREATED through Manifest's API, not only deployed through it. A person or their agent lists a project's files, reads them, and commits writes and deletions against the commit it read. It sees the project's history and what each commit changed, sets the values of the app's declared secrets, and builds exactly the commit it wrote with that commit's own `manifest.yaml`. Every operation, field, error code and event is documented well enough that a human developer and an AI agent can each drive the API from the documentation alone. The documentation is served over the API, rendered for people, and held true by gates. **It is proved by a bulletin board that an agent builds from the bare skeleton through nothing but the API, on both source drivers, and that a person then uses: a student posts and an instructor replies.**

**Architecture:** One write primitive, rebuilt. Today both drivers write files into a throwaway **worktree**, and *Read this first* 1 measured that a symlink a person pushed defeats the path check and runs a command through `core.fsmonitor`. From Task 3, **a commit is built with git plumbing and no worktree at all**: blobs by `hash-object`, a temporary index read from the base commit, `update-index -z --index-info`, `write-tree`, `commit-tree`. Every change is **planned against the base tree in TypeScript first**, because `--index-info` silently replaces (measured: writing a file named `src` deleted the whole `src/` directory). The resulting tree is **verified against the plan** before anything is pushed. The commit is then **pushed non-forced**, so driver 1's secret-scanning `pre-receive` still runs and a stale base is refused by git itself. **Both drivers share this one module**; driver 1 pushes into its bare repository and driver 2 pushes to GitHub from its mirror's objects. The API is **resources over a project's repository**: a tree, a file, a list of commits, one commit, and a commit to make. **Reads name a `ref` and writes name the `baseCommit` they were computed from.** The platform's **own record** — an audit event, never the commit's text, which anyone can forge — says who made a commit through Manifest. **App secrets are write-only**, and a deploy refuses a release whose declared secret has no value instead of dropping it. **The documentation is the contract, completed**: descriptions everywhere, an example on every operation, every error code's meaning and remedy, and every event. Hand-written guides sit beside it, their code examples real files that are type-checked and run. A reference is generated from the document. All of it is served at `/v1/docs` and `/v1/openapi.json`, shown on a console screen, and rendered as an HTML reference page.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 5.9.3 on Node **24.12.0** (host), Fastify **5.12.3**, Drizzle over Postgres 16, `zod/v4` for the API (the spec module is still `zod` **3.25.76**'s v3 API — *Read this first* 7), Vitest 2.1, `git` **2.50.1** (Apple, host) and **2.54.0** (the GitHub fake's Alpine 3.24.1). React 19.3.0 + Vite 8.3.0 in `packages/console`; `@manifest/contract` generated by `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0 and driven through `openapi-fetch` 0.17.0; Ajv 8.20.0 in `@manifest/mock`. **One new external package, at Rich's yes and with the network on: the HTML reference's renderer** (Task 1 measures the candidates — Scalar's `@scalar/api-reference` and Redoc — and picks one against stated criteria). **Two more come from this machine's store and need no network**: `zod-to-json-schema` **3.25.2** and `@redocly/openapi-core` **1.34.20** (already in the store as dependencies of other packages; Task 1 measures `pnpm add --offline` for each).

**Spec:** [`../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md`](../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md). Read these sections: **§1** (*Bring your own agent*), **§22** in full (the reference console, the journey, D23's nine principles), **§7** (the `manifest.yaml` contract — `env` entries with `secret: true`, *Sensitive fields*, *Validation*), **§14**'s first bullets (the stream, and *"Live tailing of a running application's own output is not v1"* — Spec action 1), **§20** *Credential classes (D24)*, *Machine-actionable errors* and *Git driver*, **§13** *Gate (D9)* and *Residual risk*, **§24** *Changing the answer*, **§17**'s Phase 2 and Phase 3 rows, **§6**'s `Secret` and `DelegatedToken`, and D13, D14, D22, D23, D24, D25 and D33 in §4.

**Roadmap:** the section *The authoring API* in [`2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md`](./2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md), and its rule: *anything that widens who can change a spec goes after the gate that inspects spec changes.* That gate (P6b) exists and is proved end to end. **This plan is the first thing that lets an API client write `manifest.yaml`**. Write every control as though a prompt-injected agent will arrive at it, because D14 says one will.

**Brief:** [`2026-09-19-authoring-api-brief.md`](./2026-09-19-authoring-api-brief.md). Its head carries what the D5 plan hands over. **Its §2 and §3 were read at `43f0452` and are stale** — the repository reference it calls missing is `Project.repository` since the D5 plan, and the path-escape refusal it calls "built and enforced" does not hold against a symlink (*Read this first* 1).

**Predecessor:** [`2026-09-24-d5-github-source-driver.md`](./2026-09-24-d5-github-source-driver.md), executed 2026-09-25. **Its *What this plan does not build* is an input list**: the mirror scan's cap, a validation announced to nobody, `repository.secret_refused`, a PUID in a launch-record event, and the final review's six deferred minors are Tasks 6 and 12; a driver-1 demo run on driver 2 is Task 12. **P6b's list under it** contributes `startBuild`'s spec pairing and the manifest's `blueprint:` bound to the project's pin (Task 7). It is also the house style this plan follows.

**Successor:** the vulnerability database in the console (Rich, 2026-09-24 — ORIENTATION §8, *Decided*), unless Rich places Spec action 1's plan — a running app's recent output for sandbox and staging — first (*What Rich does* 2).

## How this plan is to be executed — sittings, one per session

**One sitting per session, with a check-in at each boundary.** This pattern has carried every plan since P4a. It means a session limit never lands in the middle of a task. This plan commits after every task: a stop *between* tasks is recoverable, a stop *inside* one is not.

> 9 what the D5 plan hands over; 10 the acceptance. **The heavy ones are 2 (Task 3 rebuilds the write path on both
> drivers) and 6 (every description in the document)**: if sitting 2 runs long, stop after Task 2 and sweep, and Task 3
> opens sitting 3 ahead of Task 4. *Offered, and not chosen:* **nine** — Task 12 joins sitting 5 beside Task 8, which puts the
> inheritance in a sitting that already adds a feature; **eleven** — Task 9 split into the gate and the writing,
> which buys nothing a sitting boundary needs.

The record keeps the token WITHOUT its secret; a replay of a mint answers `409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED` naming the token the first request minted, so a client that lost the answer revokes it and mints again — D24's *shown exactly once* made true, and no second token ever minted; a custom migration scrubs the secrets already stored. 2. **Read and decide the three spec actions** (*Spec actions*, below) — **Spec action 2 before sitting 5 (DONE 2026-09-26: option (a), applied)** (Task 8 builds whichever he chooses), Spec actions 1 and 3 any time before sitting 10's sweep. **And place Spec action 1's plan**: this plan recommends directly after this one, ahead of the vulnerability database in the console, for the reason Rich gave when he placed that plan — `make refresh-vulndb` already meets the laptop's need, and an agent debugging a running app blind does not have one.

YES, 2026-09-25 — that fetch and nothing else.** Task 1 installs the candidates into a scratch directory, so that each lands in this machine's pnpm store; Task 11's `pnpm add --offline` then needs no network. The one outward action is fetching two packages from the npm registry.

6. **The conformance run's real leg, if a sitting asks** — Task 3 changes how driver 2 pushes, and `make github-conformance` against `Manifest (local dev)` checks nothing about pushing that the fake does not; **it is not owed by this plan**, and a sitting that wants it asks first.

## Read this first — what this plan knows that the roadmap and ORIENTATION §7e do not

**Read from the code at `8534e2e` and measured on this machine on 2026-09-25, while this plan was written.** **The writing session's probes are committed in [`spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/`](../spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/)** (`escape.mjs`, `plumbing.sh`, `indexinfo.sh`, `seams.sh`, `zod4port.mjs`, each with its usage on its first lines); **Task 1 re-runs each one marked *(T1: M<n>)* into `spikes/authoring-baseline/`**, where it becomes the record. **A plan is a hypothesis.**

1. **THE D5 PLAN'S PATH GUARD DOES NOT HOLD AGAINST A SYMLINK A PERSON PUSHED, AND THE HOLE IS COMMAND EXECUTION.** Both drivers commit by writing files into a throwaway worktree (`source/local-driver.ts:117-150`, `source/github/driver.ts:456-500`) behind two checks: `assertWritablePaths` refuses any path with a `.git` COMPONENT, and `resolve(work, p).startsWith(work + sep)` refuses a path that escapes. **Neither follows a symlink.** Measured with a faithful copy of the write loop on git 2.50.1, against a bare repository whose `main` a person had pushed with two symlinks — `out → <a directory outside>` and `meta → .git`: **(a)** an API write of `out/pwned.txt` created `<outside>/pwned.txt`; **(b)** an API write of `meta/config` replaced the worktree's `.git/config`, and **its `core.fsmonitor` command RAN during `git add`** (a marker file appeared). **Latent today and live the moment this plan ships**: `commitFiles`' only callers are a new project's seed and the tests (`grep -rn commitFiles src/ | grep -v test`), and a new repository holds no person's symlink. *(T1: M1.)*
2. **A PLUMBING COMMIT NEEDS NO WORKTREE, AND KEEPS EVERY PROPERTY THE WORKTREE GAVE.** In a scratch bare repository whose `objects/info/alternates` names the target's objects: `git read-tree <base>` into a temporary `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, blobs by `hash-object -w --stdin`, `update-index --cacheinfo`, `write-tree`, `commit-tree -p <base>`, then **`git push <target> <commit>:refs/heads/main`, non-forced**. **The target's `pre-receive` RAN** (it printed its stdin) — so driver 1's secret-scanning hook stays the backstop for Manifest's own commits, which ORIENTATION §8's open question on hook cost depends on. **A second commit on the old base was refused `! [rejected] … (non-fast-forward)`, exit 1** — optimistic concurrency, from git. `update-index --cacheinfo` **refused** a path under a pushed symlink (`cannot add out/pwned`). **`update-index --force-remove` fails in a bare repository** (`fatal: this operation must be run in a work tree`). *(T1: M2.)*
3. **`update-index --index-info` DELETES WITHOUT A WORKTREE — AND SILENTLY REPLACES WHERE `--cacheinfo` REFUSES.** Same scratch repository, each case from the same base, every one `exit 0`: `0 <zero sha>\tsrc/a.js` removed the file (**the deletion primitive**); **deleting a path that is not there changed nothing and said nothing**; **writing `out/pwned` replaced the symlink `out` with a directory**; **writing a FILE named `src` removed the whole `src/` directory** (`src/a.js` and `src/b.js`); writing `README.md/inner` removed the file `README.md`. **So `--index-info` is the right primitive only behind a planner that refuses each of those shapes first, and a check that the tree written is exactly the tree planned** (Decisions 1 and 2). *(T1: M3.)*
4. **`startBuild` PAIRS A GIVEN COMMIT WITH THE NEWEST SPEC — WHATEVER COMMIT THAT SPEC CAME FROM.** `api/routes/builds.ts:73-79` selects the project's newest `app_specs` row; `:108` builds `body.commitSha ?? spec.commitSha` with `appSpecId: spec.id`. So a client that commits X, then Y, then builds X gets X's code frozen, at release, with Y's `manifest.yaml` — resources, services, attributes. **P6b named it; an API that commits makes it the normal case.** (Task 7.)
5. **AN APP'S DECLARED SECRET IS DROPPED AT INJECTION, WITH NO REFUSAL AND NO WAY TO SET IT.** §7's schema accepts `env: [{ name: SIS_API_KEY, secret: true }]` — *"value held by Manifest, never in git"* — and `spec/injection.ts:392-395` renders only `resolved.env.filter((e) => e.value !== undefined)`, so the app deploys without it. No route writes a value. **The store exists**: `secrets` (`db/schema.ts:344`, unique on project, environment kind and name), envelope-encrypted, with `putSecret`/`getSecret`/`deleteSecret` (`secrets/store.ts`) and two namespaces in use — `app:sessionSecret` and `service:<name>:password`. **And the redactor already reads every secret stored for an app+environment** (`createAppSecrets(...).secretValues`), so a value stored there is redacted from Incidents with no further change. (Task 8.)
6. **THE DOCUMENTATION BASELINE, COUNTED FROM `packages/contract/openapi.json` (`1.2.0`).** **43 operations** (23 GET … by `jq` over `.paths`), **64 component schemas**, **786 schema properties of which 556 carry no `description` (71%)**, **0 examples anywhere** (`grep -c '"example'`), 34 schemas with no description, three operations whose description is a 28-character fragment (`listBuilds`, `listReleases`, `getRelease`). **`ErrorCode` is a bare enum of 99 codes with one sentence for all of them**, while `api/error-codes.ts` holds a summary for every code that never reaches the document. **And the published text has already drifted**: `SensitiveDiff` says *"D9. Reported, not yet enforced (P6)."* — P6b enforces it — and `SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE`'s summary says *"The slug resolves outside the repository root"* while the D5 plan's review made it the `.git` refusal too. **Public text also names internal artefacts** a reader outside the team cannot resolve: *"Rich's R6"*, *"P5a Task 5"*, *"the D5 plan's Decision 18"*. *(T1: M6.)*
7. **`manifest.yaml`'s SCHEMA IS ZOD 3; A JSON SCHEMA OF IT HAS TWO ROUTES.** `spec/schema.ts` imports `zod` (3.25.76's v3 API), and `spec/errors.ts` switches on v3 issue codes (`unrecognized_keys`, `:44`, `:72`), so `z.toJSONSchema` (zod/v4) cannot read it. **A literal port of its shape to `zod/v4` emitted a JSON Schema** (`runtime.build` as `{"not":{}}`, the reserved hooks as `maxItems: 0`; the `env` entry's `refine` is not representable and is dropped silently). **`zod-to-json-schema` 3.25.2 is already in the store**, and reads a v3 schema as it is. Task 1 measures both against the spec suite. *(T1: M7.)*
8. **`@redocly/openapi-core` 1.34.20 IS IN THE STORE** (a dependency of `openapi-typescript`). Its `lint` is a checker of the document **that nobody on this project wrote** — the D5 plan's Decision 7 argument, applied to prose and structure. *(T1: M8 — `pnpm add --offline` and a lint of `openapi.json` with the `recommended` rules, offline.)*
9. **THE CONSOLE'S IMPORT BOUNDARY SCANS `src/` ONLY** (`packages/console/src/boundary.test.ts`: `@manifest/contract`, `react`, `react-dom`, `node:` builtins and its own files). A second Vite page OUTSIDE `src/` can host a renderer without weakening D22's rule for the console itself, and gets its own boundary (Decision 17).
10. **THE MOCK ALREADY HOLDS ITSELF TO THE DOCUMENT**: *"has an entry for every operation the contract declares"* (`packages/mock/src/server.test.ts:14`), and every answer is validated by Ajv 2020 against the document on its way out (`validate.ts`). So an example PRINTED IN THE DOCUMENT can be what the mock answers for an operation it has no scripted fixture for — one source, validated twice (Decision 15).
11. **`defineRoute` HAS NO BODY LIMIT OF ITS OWN.** `registerRoutes` calls `app.route` without `bodyLimit`, so every `/v1` route takes Fastify's default **1 MiB** and answers `413 REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE` past it (measured by the D5 plan's `[M9]`). **An ordinary commit fits easily** — measured here: the whole `node-ts-mongo` skeleton is 104 KB on disk, its `package-lock.json` 61,066 bytes, the proof-app's `server.js` 15,541. **But a commit's body is JSON**, so ONE file at Decision 4's 1 MiB per-file limit, escaped, is already over the default, and the per-file limit would be unreachable through the route that enforces it. `RouteDefinition` gains `bodyLimit` (Task 6).
12. **NO CAPABILITY NAMES SOURCE.** `project:write` is asserted by exactly two routes — `validateSpec` and `mintToken` (`grep -rn "'project:write'" src/ | grep -v test`). A token minted today holding `project:write` would, if commits reused it, gain the power to change code **with nothing about the token changing** (Decision 8).
13. **ACTORS CARRY NO NAME.** `SessionActor` has `userId`, `platformRole`, `puid`; `TokenActor` has `userId` (the minter), `tokenId`, `projectId`, `capabilities`. **A person's name comes from `users.display_name`**, as `releases/approval.ts:86-90` reads it — the P6b F14 rule: an event's sentence names a person, never a PUID. **And `launch/records.ts:246` and `:314` still write `${input.actor.puid} recorded…`** — the D5 plan's sitting 8 F17, and its sibling it did not name. (Task 12.)
14. **§24 SAYS RAISING AN AUDIENCE IS A REQUEST AN ADMINISTRATOR APPROVES**, *"in the admin queue (§26) with the owner's justification"*, and lowering it is immediate. That is an admin-queue feature, not an authoring one — named in *What this plan does not build* with its reason.
15. **THE CONTRACT IS `1.2.0` WITH 43 OPERATIONS; AN ADDITIVE CHANGE BUMPS THE MINOR, ONCE PER PLAN** (`api/contract/document.ts`'s own comment; P6b Decision 15; the D5 plan's Decision 15). The first task whose change reaches `openapi.json` takes `1.3.0` — **predicted: Task 2**, because `ApprovalDiff.changes` is published and gains `added`/`removed`; and registering an error code alone moves the document (the D5 plan's sitting 1, F8). *(T1: M15 — predict it again.)*
16. **THE SKELETON ALONE SEEDS A VALID `manifest.yaml`** (`blueprints/seed.ts:40`): a project created with no starter is the skeleton — `server.js`, `auth/`, `ai/`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json` — and a manifest the platform writes. That is the bulletin board's starting point.

---

## Decisions this plan makes, and why

**Twenty questions below Rich's line**, made while the plan was written. Each records what it rejected and what changing course would cost.

**Decision 1. A commit is built with git plumbing and no worktree, by ONE module both drivers share.** `source/plumbing.ts` exports `planChanges` and `buildCommit`. `buildCommit` makes a scratch bare repository whose `objects/info/alternates` names the objects of the repository the base lives in — driver 1's bare repository, driver 2's mirror. It reads the base into a temporary index, writes every blob with one `git hash-object -w --no-filters --stdin-paths` over numbered files in the scratch directory (**never at the file's own path**, so there is nothing for a symlink to redirect), applies every change with one `git update-index -z --index-info`, writes the tree, checks it (Decision 2), and makes the commit with `commit-tree -F -`, the message on stdin. Each driver then **pushes that commit non-forced** to its own target: driver 1 into its bare repository, where its `pre-receive` runs; driver 2 to GitHub, through `gitWithToken`. **`commitThroughWorktree` and `commitAndPush` are deleted**, and a project's seed goes through the same path with no base. *Rejected:* **keeping the worktree and `lstat`ing every path component** — a check-then-write on a filesystem is a race, and `fsmonitor`, clean filters, `core.hooksPath` and every future git feature that reads a worktree are all reasons a worktree exists; the attack surface is the worktree itself. **`git apply` of a patch** — it too wants a worktree or an index keyed by paths, and a patch's context lines make an agent's intent fuzzy where a list of writes and deletions is exact. *Changing course* is one module, behind `SourceDriver.commit`.

**Decision 2. Every change is planned against the base tree before the index is touched, and the tree written is checked against the plan.** `planChanges(base, changes)` reads the base's full listing (`git ls-tree -r -t -l -z --full-tree`) and refuses, **each with its own code**: a write whose path is a directory in the base, or whose parent is a file, a symlink or a submodule (`409 SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT`); a write to a path the base has as a symlink or a submodule (the same code — v1 writes regular text files only); a deletion of a path the base does not have (`409 SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND`), or of a directory (`SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT` — delete its files); a path named twice (`SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT`); and a set of changes that leaves the tree as it was (`409 SOURCE_NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`). After `write-tree`, `ls-tree -r -z` of the new tree must equal the base's entries minus the deletions plus the writes, by path, mode and blob — **or the commit is abandoned** with `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED` and an operator line. **Belt and braces, deliberately**: the planner is the control, and the check is what makes a planner defect a refusal rather than a silent deletion (*Read this first* 3). *Rejected:* **letting git decide** (`--cacheinfo` refuses some shapes, but not a deletion of a path that is not there, and it needs a process per file). **Resolving conflicts in the same commit** (delete `out`, then write `out/x`) — possible, and a second commit does it; v1 refuses rather than guesses. *Changing course* is the planner.

**Decision 3. A write names the commit it was computed from, and a moved branch is `409 SOURCE_CONFLICT` — one code, both drivers, both ways it can happen.** `baseCommit` is REQUIRED. The route resolves `main` first; a base that is not `main`'s head is refused before any work, and a push git refuses as non-fast-forward (a race lost in the gap) is mapped to the same code. **`SOURCE_CONFLICT` already means exactly this** — *"GitHub's branch moved between the read and the push; read it again and retry"* (driver 2) — so its summary becomes driver-neutral rather than a second code being added. *Rejected:* **`baseCommit` optional, defaulting to the head** — an agent that did not say what it read overwrites a person's push without knowing it existed. **A three-way merge** — a merge an agent did not see is a change nobody reviewed. *Changing course* is one optional field.

**Decision 4. Text only, stated as rules, each refused with its own answer.** A write's `content` is a JSON string that is **well-formed Unicode** (`String.prototype.isWellFormed()` — a lone surrogate would be written as U+FFFD, silently), **contains no NUL** (git would call the file binary, and D5's push-time scan skips binary files), and is written as its **UTF-8 bytes, exactly**. **At most 1 MiB of UTF-8 per file**, **500 changes per commit**, **8 MiB per request body** (the route's own `bodyLimit`, Decision 11). A path is relative, `/`-separated, **at most 1024 bytes, 255 per component and 32 components deep**, with no empty, `.` or `..` component, no leading or trailing `/`, no backslash, no control character, and **no `.git` component in any case** — which the request schema refuses first, and each driver's `assertWritablePaths` refuses again as `SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE` (the D5 plan's review), two reads of one rule. **Every one of these is the request schema's** — `400 REQUEST_INVALID`, naming the change's index, the path and the rule — because each is a property of the request alone, and one code for a malformed request is D23.7's promise; the byte limit is a `superRefine` on `Buffer.byteLength`, since `z.string().max` counts UTF-16 units. **A file larger than 1 MiB can still arrive by a person's push**, and READING one through the API is `409 SOURCE_FILE_TOO_LARGE` — the source family's status, as every `SourceError` but `SOURCE_UNREACHABLE` is. **A new file is mode `100644`; an existing regular file keeps its mode**, so an executable script stays executable. **Reading a file git would call binary** (a NUL in its first 8000 bytes, git's own rule), **or one that is not valid UTF-8**, is `409 SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT` — a Latin-1 file decoded with replacement characters and committed back would be corrupted silently — and a listing marks it `binary: true`, so a client can say *"a binary file — not editable here"* rather than show garbage. *Rejected:* **base64 content for binary files** — Rich's *text files only in v1*; the representation leaves room (an optional `encoding` field is additive). *Changing course* is additive.

**Decision 5. Who made a commit is the PLATFORM's record, never the commit's text.** An API commit's **author** is the person — their display name, and `<userId>@users.manifest.internal`, **an address in the platform's own zone that is nobody's mailbox**, so neither a PUID nor a real email reaches git history (or GitHub's, on driver 2); for a token it is the minter's name with the token's in brackets — *"Ada Lovelace (via token 'claude-code')"*. The **committer** is Manifest, as today. **But attribution is read from the audit trail**: every API commit publishes `repository.committed` naming the actor (Decision 10), and `listCommits` joins those events by commit id. A commit with no such event — a person's `git push`, on either driver — is shown with **`madeThrough: null`** and git's own author name, marked as git's claim. *Rejected:* **trailers such as `Manifest-Actor:`** — anyone who can push can write one, so the history would attribute a person's push to an agent, or the reverse. **A new table** — `audit.events` is already append-only by grant, and a second record of the same fact is a second thing to keep in step. *Changing course* is the join.

**Decision 6. An API commit never leaves `manifest.yaml` invalid — and a dry run answers exactly what the commit would.** Before anything is written, the route computes the manifest the commit would produce (the write's content, or the base's file, or nothing if it is deleted) and validates it with the SAME function and context `validateAndRecord` uses — the model catalogue, the quota, the blueprint pin (Decision 12). **Invalid is `422 SPEC_INVALID` with `details`**, the code the rest of the API already answers. `dryRun: true` runs every check in the order the commit would — shape, `.git`, secrets, base, planner, manifest — and writes nothing: its `201` carries `commitSha: null`, what would change, the validation and the sensitive diff. *Rejected:* **commit and record invalid**, which is the webhook's semantics because a person's push cannot be refused; an API commit can be, and an agent that is told why corrects itself (D23.7). *Changing course* is one branch.

**Decision 7. Every validation publishes `spec.validated`** — `POST …/spec`, a pushed `main` (the webhook), and every API commit (the D5 plan's *Added at the close*: *"a validation announced to nobody"*). `validateAndRecord` publishes it, so the three callers cannot disagree. The payload is unchanged (`appSpecId`, `commitSha`, `valid`, `errorCount`); the sentence says what was validated and whether it passed. **A client learns from the stream that a person's push left `manifest.yaml` invalid**, which is the case that mattered. *Changing course* is one call.

**Decision 8. Writing source is its own capability, `source:write`.** Owner, collaborator and platform administrator hold it; **it can be minted into a token**; it is **neither privileged nor person-only**. Reading source is `project:read`, as every other read of a project is. *Rejected:* **reusing `project:write`** (*Read this first* 12). **Making it privileged**, which puts D24's confirm-and-retry loop in front of every commit an agent makes — authoring would be unusable, and §13's gate holds what the spec says it holds: the sensitive fields at production, re-escalated to an administrator (P6b). **The residual risk is §13's own, stated there**: the gate reviews `manifest.yaml`, not code, and D33's reviewer is a null implementation. *Changing course* is the matrix.

**Decision 9. Reads name a `ref`; writes go to `main`.** `ref` is a branch name or a full 40-character commit id, and defaults to `main`; every read answers the commit it resolved, so a client knows exactly what it read. **Writes target `main` only in v1, and the request has no branch field** — adding an optional one later is additive (D23.8), and D14's branch-scoped credential is a sandbox's (Phase 3). *Rejected:* **a branch field now**, which invites a preview-deploy story this plan does not build.

**Decision 10. Two new events, and one published everywhere.** **`repository.committed`** `{ commitSha, parent, added, modified, deleted, via: 'session' | 'token', tokenId: uuid | null }`, its sentence naming the person (and the token by name) and the first line of the message, cut at 72 characters. **`repository.secret_refused`** `{ findings: [{ path, line, rule }] }` — **never the value**, published when a real commit (not a dry run) is refused `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, so an owner learns an agent tried to commit a credential. **`spec.validated`** on every validation (Decision 7). Each is the four edits `EVENT_TYPES`, the `CHECK` (a migration), `EVENT_DETAIL_SCHEMAS` and `EXAMPLE_DETAILS`. *Rejected:* **an event for every refused commit** — a stale base or a typo is the client's business, not the audit trail's; a secret is the platform's. **Publishing on a dry run** — probing is not an attempt, and its answer already names the finding.

**Decision 11. `defineRoute` gains `bodyLimit`, and only `createCommit` uses it — 8 MiB.** The per-file limit is 1 MiB of UTF-8 and a JSON string escapes (a newline is two bytes, a quote two), so the route must admit a body larger than its largest file; 8 MiB admits several files at the limit and bounds the parse. Every other route keeps Fastify's 1 MiB. *Rejected:* **raising the server's default**, which widens every route's parser for one route's need; **a per-file limit under 1 MiB so the default suffices**, which refuses a legitimate data file for the sake of a parser setting. *Changing course* is one number.

**Decision 12. A build uses the validation of the commit it builds; a manifest may not name another blueprint.** `startBuild` with a `commitSha` uses the newest `app_specs` row **for that commit**, validating it first if there is none (`validateAndRecord` at that commit), and refuses `422 SPEC_INVALID` for an invalid one. With no `commitSha` it keeps today's meaning — the newest recorded validation's commit — which the guide states (ORIENTATION §4 trap 23). **And validation refuses a `blueprint:` that is not the project's pin** — `SPEC_BLUEPRINT_NOT_PINNED`, a `ManifestError` in `details` — because an agent that writes `manifest.yaml` could otherwise point the build at another blueprint's knowledge pack while `startBuild` reads the project's (P6b's list). *Rejected:* **refusing only at build time** (`checkBlueprintCompatibility` compares versions, not names), because a commit that is refused later than it could be is an agent's wasted build.

**Decision 13. App secrets are write-only, stored beside the platform's own, and a deploy refuses what it cannot render.** Three operations on an environment: `GET …/secrets` lists **names** — every one the environment's newest valid spec declares with `secret: true` (with `environments.<kind>.env` folded over the top level, as `resolveConfig` does), and every one set — each with `declared`, `set` and `updatedAt`; `PUT …/secrets/{name}` sets a value; `DELETE …/secrets/{name}` clears it. **No operation ever answers a value**, and `secret:read` stays out of `Capability` (D24; P5b Decision 14). A value is **1 to 16384 UTF-8 bytes, well-formed, with no NUL**; a name is §7's `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$`, at most 128 characters, and **not one of `RESERVED_ENV_NAMES`** (`400 SECRET_NAME_RESERVED` — its own code, because an agent that is told *that name is the platform's* stops asking; the value's rules are the request schema's `400 REQUEST_INVALID`). A value may be set before the manifest declares its name (`declared: false`) — an owner often sets a key first — and **only a declared name is ever rendered**. Stored as `env:<NAME>` in `secrets`, the third namespace beside `app:` and `service:`, so the redactor covers it with no change (*Read this first* 5). **`deployRelease` refuses a release whose frozen config declares a secret that has no value in that environment — `409 RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET`, naming the names and never a value — before an instance row, a service or a network exists**, like the digest check. The rehearsal deploys through the same function and inherits it. A value takes effect **at the next deploy**; setting one redeploys nothing. `secret:write` is a new capability — owner, collaborator, administrator; mintable — and **production is Spec action 2's answer**: as recommended, a production value is set only in an interactive session with step-up (`requireSession` + `assertStepUp`), while sandbox and staging take a token. Events `app_secret.set` and `app_secret.cleared` `{ environmentKind, name }` name the person. *Rejected:* **a readable secret for its owner**, which is D24's `secret read` by another name. **Silently deploying without it** — what happens today. *Changing course* is the production branch.

**Decision 14. The document IS the reference, and completeness is a gate over the generated document, not a convention.** `api/contract/docs.test.ts` walks `openApiDocument(ROUTE_DEFINITIONS)` and refuses: an operation with no `description` of at least one sentence, or no **example** (`request` for a body, `response` always); a parameter, schema or property with no `description`; an error code with no **meaning** and **remedy**; an event type with no description or example; and **public text naming an internal artefact** (`/\b(P[1-6][abc]?|sitting|Task \d+|Decision \d+|Rich)\b|the D5 plan|R[1-9]\b/`). Descriptions come from zod's `.describe()` on requests and representations, lifted onto path and query parameters by `document.ts`. **Examples are part of `RouteDefinition`** — `examples: { request?: z.input<B>; response: z.input<R> }` — and the gate PARSES each one through its own schema, so an example that has drifted from its schema is red. **Error codes gain `remedy`** in `api/error-codes.ts` beside `summary`, and the document publishes both as `x-enumDescriptions` on `ErrorCode` and as a top-level `x-manifest-errors` table — `x-enumDescriptions` keeps the generated `ErrorCode` union identical, where a `oneOf` of `const`s would change `schema.d.ts`. **Events** are documented on the stream's path item, one entry per type with its description and `EXAMPLE_DETAILS`' example. **And an independent linter**, `@redocly/openapi-core`'s `recommended` rules, runs over `openapi.json` in the unit tier, with each disabled rule named and justified. *Rejected:* **a completeness review by reading** — 556 undescribed properties are what reading produced. *Changing course* is the gate's allowlist, which starts and ends EMPTY.

**Decision 15. The mock answers a new operation with the document's own example.** `packages/mock/src/server.ts` answers `ANSWERS[operationId] ?? exampleOf(operation)`, where `exampleOf` reads the operation's success example out of the document it already loads; its gate becomes *"every operation has a scripted answer or a document example"*. The existing fixtures stay — they script states the examples do not (P5c Decision 9). *Rejected:* **a second hand-written fixture per new operation**, which is two statements of one answer.

**Decision 16. The guides are Markdown in `docs/api/`, and every code example in them is a real file that is type-checked and run.** Hand-written pages explain; their code blocks are **inlined** from `packages/journey/src/examples/*.ts` between `<!-- example: <name> -->` markers by `pnpm docs:write`, and those files are type-checked by `pnpm typecheck` and **run against `manifest-mock`** by `examples.test.ts`. The reference pages (`docs/api/reference/*.md`) are **generated** from the document. `docs/api/docs.test.ts` holds three things: the committed pages equal what `pnpm docs:write` would write (drift); every backticked `operationId`, error code and event type a hand-written page names exists; and every relative link resolves. *Rejected:* **code blocks written in the Markdown** — an example nothing runs is the drift this project has paid for, in prose. **A documentation site generator** — a dependency and a build for what the control plane can serve as text.

**Decision 17. The HTML reference is a second console page outside `src/`, with its own boundary.** `packages/console/reference.html` and `packages/console/reference/main.ts` mount the renderer Task 1 chose over `/v1/openapi.json`, which the browser fetches with its own session on the console's origin. Vite builds both pages. `src/` keeps D22's rule unchanged; **`reference/` may import the renderer and nothing else**, held by its own test in `boundary.test.ts`. **The renderer is configured offline** — no web fonts, no telemetry, no external request — and Task 1 measures that from the browser's network log. *Rejected:* **serving it from the control plane** — the edge forwards only `/v1/*` and `/auth/*` to 7100, and a second static server is a second thing on the platform; **a page inside `src/`**, which is exactly the exemption D22's gate exists to refuse.

**Decision 18. The documentation is served over the API, as the knowledge pack is (D25, D23.4).** `GET /v1/docs` answers the index (each page's `slug`, `title`, `summary` and `audience`), `GET /v1/docs/{slug}` a page's Markdown, and `GET /v1/openapi.json` the document — **generated from `ROUTE_DEFINITIONS` at boot**, the same function the drift test compares with the committed file, so what is served is what is published. **Any credential may read them** — a session, or a token of any project — because documentation is not a project's data; anonymous is `401`, as every `/v1` route is. `llms.txt` is generated beside the pages and served by the console's origin at `/llms.txt` (a file in `packages/console/public/`), where the convention puts it. `loadApiDocs` reads `docs/api/` at boot, bounded, and **a missing or unreadable page refuses the boot, naming it** — as a broken blueprint does. *Rejected:* **the repository as the only copy** — a bring-your-own agent on someone's laptop has no checkout, which is D25's argument exactly.

**Decision 19. The journey is data, and its coverage is a gate.** `packages/journey/src/coverage.ts` states §22's end-to-end path as steps, each naming the `operationId`s that serve it, or `later: '<plan or phase>'` with the reason. `coverage.test.ts` refuses a named operation the document lacks, and **an operation the document has that no step names** — unless it is in `OUTSIDE_THE_JOURNEY` with a reason (the fleet, the admin's queue). `docs/api/journey.md`'s table is generated from it. This is the converse of D22's console gate, for the whole path rather than one client. *Rejected:* **the journey as prose only**, which is how *"the API can deploy and not create"* stayed true for a phase.

**Decision 20. The acceptance runs on BOTH drivers, on two projects.** A project keeps its provider (the D5 plan's Decision 3), so `make demo-authoring` asks which driver answers — an unsigned `POST /webhooks/github` is `404 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` on driver 1 and `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING` on driver 2 — and uses `board-local` or `board-github`. Task 13 runs it green on each, restarting the control plane between. **This is the promise the placement after D5 was made for**: API commits reach GitHub from the start. *Rejected:* **driver 1 only**, which leaves the second implementation of the rebuilt write path proved by the contract suite alone.

---

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section. Values are copied verbatim from the spec, or from a dated measurement.

- **Four gates, all clean before every commit**, from the **repository root**: `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm format:check`. **Run `pnpm test` twice**, because a suite that is not repeatable has a state leak. Never run it with `--filter`. **The baseline is `pnpm test` 2005 passed in 145 files, `pnpm test:docker` 208 in 34, `make doctor` 20/0/0, `make verify` 57/0/0, and 29 migrations** (ORIENTATION §2, the D5 plan's sitting 8, 2026-09-25) — **this plan's starting line, kept as written; the CURRENT baseline is always ORIENTATION §2's box.** `pnpm test` is ~6 minutes a run.
- **`pnpm test:docker`** (~22 min, needs `make up` and the chat model warm, **fails rather than skips**) is owed by any change to `runtime/`, `routing/`, `services/`, `build/`, `releases/`, `identity/`, `sso/`, `secrets/`, `projects/`, `blueprints/`, `spec/`, `ai/`, `observability/`, `launch/`, `source/`, `infra/`, or any `*.docker.test.ts`. **It outlasts the agent's 10-minute tool limit: run it in the background.** It restarts the edge, truncates the tables and re-registers the platform's SP row — **restart the control plane afterwards**, then run the three cleanup scripts, then `make verify` (twice in the D5 plan it left the host unable to reach the edge; `docker restart manifest-caddy` fixed it). Warm the model first: `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/generate -d '{"model":"qwen3.5:4b","prompt":"ok","stream":false,"think":false,"keep_alive":"30m","options":{"num_predict":1}}'`.
- **`pnpm test -- <filter>` does not filter.** One file, from the repository root: a unit file `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit src/<path>`; a packages file `pnpm exec vitest run --project packages packages/<pkg>/src/<path>`; a Docker file `MANIFEST_TEST_DOCKER=1 pnpm exec vitest run --project docker src/<path>`. **Never run two Vitest processes at once.**
- **`pnpm test` TRUNCATES the control plane's tables** — so does one file, and so does `pnpm contract:write`. **Run anything that needs a demo's rows BEFORE any Vitest run.**
- **Vitest strips types; it does not check them.** `pnpm typecheck` is the only gate that sees a whole class of error. `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on: an optional field is a conditional spread.
- **A route change is three files, in order**: the definition, then `pnpm contract:write`, then `pnpm contract:generate`. **`packages/contract/openapi.json` and `src/schema.d.ts` are generated; never edit them.** Registering an error code, an event type or a representation field moves the document too.
- **A migration is `pnpm --filter @manifest/control-plane db:generate`, then READ what it wrote.** Drizzle rewrites the `audit.events` `CHECK` itself when `db/schema.ts`'s literal changes; **appending it by hand makes the migration fail to apply**. `db:migrate` needs RUNBOOK's export block (ORIENTATION §3). **Migration numbers here are predictions** (0029 onwards); take what drizzle writes.
- **An EVENT TYPE is four edits and published surface**: `EVENT_TYPES` (`observability/events.ts`), its payload schema (`observability/event-schemas.ts`), the `CHECK` literal in `db/schema.ts`, and `EXAMPLE_DETAILS` (`observability/examples.ts` since sitting 6; `testing.ts` re-exports it) — **and since Task 9 a `.describe()` sentence on its map entry and on every field, which the reference's gate refuses without**. **A payload never carries a secret's value, a file's content, a token or a laptop path.** A sentence names a person by name, never by PUID.
- **EVERY REFUSAL ASSERTS ITS CODE, NEVER ITS STATUS ALONE** — `refusal()` from `api/testing.ts`. This plan adds `409 SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT`, `SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND`, `SOURCE_NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`, `SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT`, `SOURCE_PATH_NOT_A_FILE`, `SOURCE_REF_NOT_FOUND`, `SOURCE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`, `409 RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET`, `400 SECRET_NAME_RESERVED`, `404 DOC_NOT_FOUND`, and the `ManifestError` code `SPEC_BLUEPRINT_NOT_PINNED`; it reuses `SOURCE_CONFLICT`, `SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE`, `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, `SPEC_INVALID` and `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`. **Every new code is thrown as a literal** (`error-codes.test.ts` finds codes as `new SourceError('LITERAL'` and its siblings).
- **A REFUSAL TEST NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL IN THE SAME FILE**, and so does a negative claim. *"Refuses a write under a symlink"* is true of a driver that refuses every write.
- **Never accept a check you have not watched fail.** Every task ends by breaking what it built, **after committing the task**, and naming the test that goes red with its assertion quoted. **Predict what turns red before you run it.** A control that stays green is a question to chase, not a result.
- **Every task names its CALLER.** A module with no call site is not built.
- **No file content, secret value, token or commit message in an operator line, an error message or an event, beyond what this plan names** (a commit message's first line, cut at 72 characters, in `repository.committed`'s sentence). `SourceError` messages go on the wire.
- **Tests that run `git` set their own identity and ignore the machine's config**: `-c user.name=… -c user.email=… -c commit.gpgsign=false`, and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` wherever git reads configuration the test did not write.
- **Nothing in this plan installs an external package except the HTML renderer, at Rich's yes.** `zod-to-json-schema` and `@redocly/openapi-core` are added with `pnpm add --offline` from the store (Task 1 measures that it works). If a task believes it needs anything else, record a finding and raise it.
- **Ask before `sudo`.** No task needs it. **Never edit the spec** — Spec actions 1–3 go to Rich, and are applied only after he has read the words. **Never touch Laravel Valet.** These four containers must survive: `docker-simple-saml-saml-idp-1`, `qdrant-local-dev`, `mongodb`, `mongo-express`. **`caddy-data` must never be destroyed.**
- **macOS ships bash 3.2 and a BSD userland**: no associative arrays, no `mapfile`, no `xargs -r`, no `readlink -f`, `sed -i ''`. **The tool shell is zsh**: run multi-line probes with `bash <file>`, and never name a loop variable `path`.
- **`request.log` writes nothing** under `Fastify({ logger: false })`. Use `console.error`, never with a secret, a token, a cookie or a file's content in it. **A swallowed `.catch(() => undefined)` is this codebase's most productive defect.** **`.map(fn)` passes the ARRAY INDEX as its second argument.**
- **COMMIT ON `main`, AND STAGE YOUR OWN PATHS BY NAME**, meaning files, never directories. **Where a commit step says `<each path in Files, by name>`, it means exactly that**: every file the task's **Files** list names, plus a generated migration and its `drizzle/meta/` files, plus `pnpm-lock.yaml` when a task adds a package, plus `openapi.json` and `schema.d.ts` when the contract moved. **Never `git add -A`, `git add .`, `git commit -a` or `git checkout .`.** Run `git status` before each commit and account for every path; other sessions commit on `main` while you work. End every commit message with the attribution lines the session's own system reminder gives.
- **`$SCRATCH` IS YOUR SESSION'S SCRATCHPAD AND YOU MUST SET IT.**
  ```bash
  export SCRATCH=<your session's scratchpad directory>
  [ -d "$SCRATCH" ] || { echo 'SCRATCH is not a directory'; exit 1; }
  ```
- **Leave the machine as you found it.** `./scripts/snapshot-machine.sh` at the start and the end of a sitting, and `diff` the two. **At every close, run `bash scripts/dead-app-resources.sh`, `bash scripts/litellm-orphans.sh` and `bash scripts/app-images.sh` bare, then TRY each with `--apply` yourself**; hand the output to Rich only if you are refused. A sitting that started the GitHub fake and did not find it running stops it (`make github-down`) and says so.
- **The control plane runs ONE source driver.** Every demo but `make demo-github` and `make demo-authoring` on driver 2 is driver 1's; **a driver-1 demo run on driver 2 creates its project there for ever** (TRAPS). Until Task 12 makes each demo refuse the wrong driver, check `grep '"source"' <boot line>` before running one.

## Review Focus

**The five failure modes the spec implies and no task's happy path exercises, most likely first.** Each has its test added to the task that owns the code.

1. **A symlink, a directory, or a submodule where a write or a deletion lands** — pushed by a person, found by an agent's commit. Expected: **`409 SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT` (or `SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND`), `main` unmoved, nothing written outside the repository, and no command run** — with a canary file for `core.fsmonitor` that must not appear. Owned by Task 3's contract cases *refuses a write under a symlink a person pushed, and writes nothing outside*, *refuses a write through a symlink to .git, and runs no command*, and *refuses a file where a directory is and a directory where a file is*.
2. **Two writers racing on `main`** — an agent's commit and a person's push, or two agents. Expected: **exactly one lands; the other is `409 SOURCE_CONFLICT`**, whether the base was already stale or the race was lost at the push. Owned by Task 3's *refuses a stale base with SOURCE_CONFLICT, and main does not move* and *loses a race at the push as SOURCE_CONFLICT — exactly one of two commits lands*, and Task 6's *refuses a stale base*.
3. **An idempotent retry of a commit** — the same `Idempotency-Key`, after a timeout the client could not see the end of. Expected: **the first `201` replayed, one commit on `main`, one `repository.committed` event.** Owned by Task 6's *a retried commit — the same Idempotency-Key — is one commit and one event*.
4. **A secret-shaped value reaching the platform through the two new write paths** — a file's content in a commit, and a secret's value in a build log or an Incident after it is set. Expected: **`409 SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED` and `repository.secret_refused` with no value in its JSON**; and a set secret's value **redacted** from an Incident's `log_tail`. Owned by Task 6's *refuses a secret, names where and which rule, and publishes the finding — never the value* and Task 8's Docker case *renders a set value into the container, and redacts it from an Incident*.
5. **Documentation that has drifted from the API** — an example that no longer parses, a guide naming an operation that was renamed, a generated page older than the document. Expected: **`pnpm test` red, naming the page and the name.** Owned by Task 9's *every example parses through its own schema* and Task 11's *every name a guide mentions exists* and *the committed pages are what docs:write writes*.

## The fixtures and helpers every snippet below uses

- **`withProjectServer(fn)`** (`api/testing.ts:964`): a fresh database, a server, `bio_prof` as owner of a project created **through the route**, a second project owned by `unrelated_user`, and `ctx.commitSha` — the seed's validated commit. **`sessionFor(ctx, puid, role?, { steppedUp })`**, **`mutationHeaders(deps)`**, **`refusal(res)`** and **`loginAs(deps, puid, { steppedUp })`** are beside it.
- **`describeSourceDriver(name, make, extra)`** (`source/driver-contract.ts`): the contract every driver runs unchanged — driver 1 in `local-driver.test.ts`, driver 2 in `github/driver.test.ts` against the in-process fake. Its harness has `pushAsPerson(slug, files, message)`; **Task 3 adds `pushSymlinkAsPerson(slug, path, target, message)`** to both harnesses.
- **`SAMPLE_SECRETS`** (`build/testing.ts:16`): secret-shaped values built at run time, never a real one.
- **A token in a test** is minted through the route or written with `tokenFor(actor, projectId, capabilities)` as `api/authz-contract.ts` does; **check which of the two states your control is in** — a token written to the store can hold what no route would mint (ORIENTATION §3).
- **`$SCRATCH`** as in *Global Constraints*.

## Task 1: Measure what this plan rests on — before any of it is built

**ALONE, AND FIRST.** Every item in *Read this first* marked *(T1: M<n>)* is re-run here, and the rest are measured for the first time. **Nothing under `packages/` changes in this task.** The record is `spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md` — one section per measurement, its command, its raw answer, and what it confirms or corrects in this plan — with every command's full output in `results-task1-<date>.txt` beside it, and every probe in `probes/`.

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md`, `results-task1-<date>.txt`, `probes/*`
- Modify: this plan — a `[M<n>]` block at the head of every task a measurement corrects, and the sittings table if a boundary moves (**and then ask Rich before sitting 2** — *How this plan is to be executed*)

**Interfaces:** Produces the facts Tasks 2–13 rely on. Consumes nothing.

- [ ] **Step 0: The machine, before anything.** `./scripts/snapshot-machine.sh > "$SCRATCH/before.txt"`; `uptime` (a load over ~10 makes every timing in this task suspect — ORIENTATION §4 trap 4); `open -a Docker` if it is not running; `make up`; the four gate numbers (`make doctor`, `make verify`, `pnpm test` once, `pnpm test:docker` in the background) against the Global Constraints' baseline. **A different number is signal**: find out what moved before measuring anything else. *(M13.)*

- [ ] **Step 1: The write path's two holes, re-measured on the real drivers — `[M1]`.** Run the committed `probes/escape.mjs` (`node docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/escape.mjs "$SCRATCH"` — it copies `commitThroughWorktree`'s loop). Then **drive the real driver 1** with a scratch script under Node 24's type stripping (the `packages/github-fake` resolve hook, `--import`ed, resolves the repository's `./x.js` specifiers): `createLocalSourceDriver("$SCRATCH/repos")`, `createRepository('escape-lab', {...})`, push a person's commit carrying `out → $SCRATCH/outside` and `meta → .git` straight into the bare repository, then `commitFiles(ref, { 'out/pwned.txt': 'x' }, …)` and `commitFiles(ref, { 'meta/config': '[core]\n\tfsmonitor = touch <marker>; false\n' }, …)`. **Predict: `<outside>/pwned.txt` exists and `<marker>` exists** — the hole is in the shipped driver, not only in the copy. Record both, and git's version. **Do not run this against anything but a repository under `$SCRATCH`.**

- [ ] **Step 2: The plumbing commit, and `--index-info`'s shapes — `[M2]`, `[M3]`.** Run the committed `probes/plumbing.sh`, then `probes/indexinfo.sh` with the same directory, then `probes/seams.sh`. **Predict exactly *Read this first* 2 and 3**: the hook runs on a plumbing push; a stale base is `non-fast-forward`; `--force-remove` needs a worktree; `--index-info` deletes, and silently replaces in the three shapes listed. Add one case: **`git hash-object -w --no-filters --stdin-paths`** over numbered files in a directory holding a `.gitattributes` of `* filter=evil` — predict the blob ids equal `git hash-object --no-filters` of the same bytes and that no filter runs (there is no configuration that defines one, and `--no-filters` says so twice).

- [ ] **Step 3: The documentation baseline — `[M6]`.** Re-run the counts with these commands, into the results file:
  ```bash
  D=packages/contract/openapi.json
  jq '.info.version, (.paths|to_entries|map(.value|keys|length)|add), (.components.schemas|keys|length)' $D
  jq '[.. | objects | select(has("properties")) | .properties | to_entries[]] | length' $D
  jq '[.. | objects | select(has("properties")) | .properties | to_entries[] | select(.value.description==null)] | length' $D
  grep -c '"example' $D
  jq '.components.schemas.ErrorCode.enum|length' $D
  grep -nE '\b(P[1-6][abc]?|sitting|Task [0-9]+|Decision [0-9]+|Rich)\b|the D5 plan|\bR[1-9]\b' $D | wc -l
  ```
  **Predict 43 / 64 / 786 / 556 / 0 / 99**, and record the last count — **the number of lines of public text naming an internal artefact**, which Task 9's gate brings to zero.

- [ ] **Step 4: A JSON Schema for `manifest.yaml` — `[M7]`.** In `$SCRATCH`, `pnpm add --offline zod-to-json-schema@3.25.2` into a throwaway package (**predict: installs with no network**, because it is in the store). Emit `manifestSchema` (imported from `spec/schema.ts` under type stripping) and record: its size; how `runtime.build` (`z.never().optional()`), the reserved hooks (`z.array(z.never()).max(0)`) and the `env` entry's `.refine` come out; whether `default`s are present. **Decision rule, stated now:** use `zod-to-json-schema` (no change to how §7 validates) unless it cannot represent a field an agent writes; then — and only then — port `spec/schema.ts` to `zod/v4` in Task 9, with `spec/errors.ts`'s issue-code switch checked against v4's codes by the spec suite.

- [ ] **Step 5: An independent linter, offline — `[M8]`.** In `$SCRATCH`, `pnpm add --offline @redocly/openapi-core@1.34.20`. Lint `packages/contract/openapi.json` with its `recommended` ruleset (`lintFromString` with `createConfig({ extends: ['recommended'] })`, or whatever 1.34.20's API names are — read its `lib/index.d.ts`, never guess). Record **every rule that fires, with its count**. Task 9 either fixes each or disables it in `api/contract/lint.test.ts` with a sentence saying why; **none is disabled silently**.

- [ ] **Step 6: The HTML renderer — `[M9]`. NETWORK, AT RICH'S YES, AND ONLY FOR THIS STEP.** Ask Rich. With his yes, in `$SCRATCH`, install the two candidates at their current versions and record the exact versions: **`@scalar/api-reference`** and **`redoc`**. **Judge each OFFLINE by its network log, with the network left ON** — an agent cannot turn the network off from a tool call without cutting itself off (CLAUDE.md), and it does not need to: a request to any host but the page's own is in Chrome's network log whether or not it would have succeeded (C1). For each, a static page loading the package's standalone browser bundle over `packages/contract/openapi.json`, opened in Chrome with its network log open (the claude-in-chrome extension's `read_network_requests`, or DevTools), **every request's host recorded**. Record:

  | Criterion | Kind |
  |---|---|
  | **Zero requests to any host but the page's own** — in the network log — with the candidate's own offline configuration (fonts, telemetry, CDN assets) | **hard** |
  | **Every operation and component of the 3.1 document renders** without an error (zod's `anyOf` with `null`, `const`, `x-manifest-websocket`) | **hard** |
  | Examples shown; `x-enumDescriptions` shown on `ErrorCode` | soft |
  | Licence (both are believed MIT — read it) | hard |
  | Bundle size; whether it needs a build step or loads as one file | soft |

  **Predict Scalar**, configured with its default fonts off, because it is 3.1-native. **Pick by the criteria, not the prediction**, and record the loser's reason. Leave the chosen version in the store; Task 11's `pnpm add --offline <pkg>@<version>` then needs no network.

- [ ] **Step 7: A tree's cost at 10,000 files, and the build context on a very large tree — `[M10]`, the brief's §7.** In a scratch bare repository, commit 10,000 small files across 200 directories plus one 20 MB text file. Time, three runs each: `git ls-tree -r -t -l -z --full-tree HEAD`; `git diff --numstat 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904 HEAD` (git's binary flags in one command — the empty tree's id); `git archive HEAD | wc -c` (what `build/context.ts` does). **Predict each under 1 s except the archive's bytes**, and record what Task 4's tree cap (10,000 entries) and Task 4's diff cap (256 KiB of patch) should be if the prediction is wrong. **The build context has no size bound in v1** — record the archive size and time as the number *What this plan does not build* names.

- [ ] **Step 8: `exec` against a real container — the brief's §7.** `docker run -d --rm --name mf-exec-probe manifest-registry…/base/node@<the blueprint's digest> sleep 300` (**a name no cleanup script mistakes for an app's**), then drive `runtime/docker/exec.ts`'s `containerExec` once from a scratch script with the resolve hook: `['sh', '-c', 'echo out; echo err >&2; exit 3']`. Record stdout, stderr, the exit code, and whether the stream ends. `docker rm -f mf-exec-probe`. **This plan does not build on `exec`** (S5's) — the answer goes to S5's brief, as the authoring brief asked.

- [ ] **Step 9: Predictions for the plan's own seams — `[M15]`.** Write down, before sitting 2 starts, **which task first moves `openapi.json`** (predicted: **Task 2** — `ApprovalDiff.changes` is published and gains `added`/`removed`), and **which existing tests `commit` replacing `commitFiles` breaks** (predicted: the **eighteen** call sites `grep -rn commitFiles src/` lists outside the interface and the two implementations — `source/driver-contract.ts` ×6, `api/projects.test.ts` ×5, `api/testing.ts` ×2, `source/github/driver.test.ts` ×2, and one each in `source/local-driver.test.ts`, `api/delivery.test.ts` and `releases/approval.test.ts`; counted while this plan was written). A wrong prediction is a finding.

- [ ] **Step 10: The record, and the machine.** Write `README.md`; correct every task a measurement contradicts with a dated `[M<n>]` block at its head; `diff` a fresh snapshot against `before.txt` (the throwaway container gone, no scratch repository outside `$SCRATCH`, the network as Rich left it). Commit:
  ```bash
  git add docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/results-task1-<date>.txt docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/<each probe, by name> docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-authoring-api.md
  ```

## Task 2: F7 — an attribute change's added and removed halves, handed to the model as their own facts, and adopted only if it measures better

> **`[M15]` — CONFIRMED, AND ONE PATH CORRECTED (2026-09-25, sitting 1, F15; `spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md`).**
> This task is the first to move `openapi.json` **whatever Step 5 decides**: `ApprovalDiff.changes[]` is published inline as
> `{ from, path, summary, to }`, and Step 5 keeps the published `added`/`removed` even when the prompt change is reverted.
> **Every command naming `spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/f7.mjs` resolves only from `docs/superpowers/`** — from the
> repository root it is `node docs/superpowers/spikes/authoring-baseline/probes/f7.mjs 40 2` (and `… 40 3`). Its parent,
> `spikes/d5-baseline/probes/structured.mjs`, exists and imports the shipped `summary.ts` under Node's type stripping; if
> `f7.mjs`'s import graph ever reaches a class with a parameter property (`SourceError` is one), strip-only mode refuses it
> and `--experimental-transform-types` loads it (F1).

**Rich's decision, 2026-09-25: this is the first task after the measurements.** The D5 plan's sitting 7 measured **5 of 40** `auth.attributes` sentences saying the app now RECEIVES `sn` when the change removes it. The model is handed `from: "eduPersonAffiliation, givenName, mail, sn, ubcEduCwlPuid"` and `to: "eduPersonAffiliation, givenName, mail, ubcEduCwlPuid"` and must work out the difference itself. **The fix to try**: every change to a set-valued path carries `added` and `removed` as lists, computed by `describeDiff`, and the model is handed them. **Adopted only if the measurement says so** (Step 5); otherwise reverted, and options (b) and (c) of the D5 plan's sitting 7 go back to Rich.

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/spec/diff.ts` — `SpecChange` gains optional `added`/`removed`; `setChange`, `servicesChange` and `envChange` fill them

## Task 3: The write primitive, rebuilt — plumbing commits with no worktree, planned against the base tree, verified, and pushed non-forced, on both drivers

> **`[M1]`, `[M2]`, `[M3]` AND `[M15]` — CONFIRMED, WITH TWO THINGS ADDED (2026-09-25, sitting 1, F3 and F12;
> `spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md`).** Both holes are in the SHIPPED driver 1, not only in the copy
> (`probes/driver1-escape.ts`) — **and in both cases `commitFiles` THREW `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED` AFTER the damage was done**:
> the file outside was written before git refused to add it, and through `meta → .git` `git add` ran the `fsmonitor`
> command, `git commit` SUCCEEDED, and only the push failed, because the replaced config had lost `remote.origin`. So this
> task's attack cases must go on asserting the FILESYSTEM and the canary, never the refusal alone — a refusal is no evidence
> that nothing happened. **Every plumbing prediction held exactly** (`plumbing.sh`, `indexinfo.sh`, `seams.sh`). **The
> `hash-object` filter case now has the positive control it lacked** (`probes/filters.sh`): with a clean filter DEFINED, a
> worktree runs it without `--no-filters` (the blob ids change, the marker appears) and not with it; in the bare
> `--git-dir` shape this task builds it does not run even WITHOUT the flag — so `--no-filters` is a second layer that
> holds alone. **The eighteen call sites are exactly the plan's.** *A scratch probe against `source/`* runs as `node
> --experimental-transform-types --import ./packages/github-fake/resolve-ts.mjs <probe>.ts` with `MANIFEST_DATABASE_URL`
> pointed at an address nothing listens on (F1, F2).

**This task closes *Read this first* 1 by removing the thing that has the hole.** After it, nothing in the control plane writes a repository's file at that file's own path.

  ```ts
  export type Change =

  export interface CommitRequest {
  ```

  ```ts
  const blob = (mode = '100644'): BaseEntry => ({ mode, type: 'blob', sha: 'a'.repeat(40), size: 1 })
  const tree: BaseEntry = { mode: '040000', type: 'tree', sha: 'b'.repeat(40), size: null }
  const BASE = new Map<string, BaseEntry>([
    ['README.md', blob()],
    ['run.sh', blob('100755')],
    ['src', tree],
    ['src/a.js', blob()],
    ['out', blob('120000')],            // a symlink a person pushed
    ['meta', blob('120000')],           // a symlink to .git a person pushed
    ['vendor', { mode: '160000', type: 'commit', sha: 'c'.repeat(40), size: null }],

      expect(refused(() => planChanges(BASE, [{ op: 'write', path: 'meta/config', content: 'x' }]))).toBe('SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT')
  ```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `plumbing.ts`.**
  ```ts
  import { spawn } from 'node:child_process'
  import { mkdir, mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
  import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
  import { join } from 'node:path'
  import {
    type Change,
    type ChangeStatus,
    type GitIdentity,
    SourceError,
  } from './git-driver.js'

  /** Manifest commits as itself; a PERSON is the author (the authoring API plan's Decision 5). */
  export const MANIFEST_COMMITTER: GitIdentity = {
    name: 'Manifest',
    email: 'manifest@manifest.internal',
  }

  const ZERO = '0'.repeat(40)
  const REGULAR = new Set(['100644', '100755'])

  /** One entry of `git ls-tree -r -t -l -z`: a tree, a blob (a file or a symlink) or a submodule. */
  export interface BaseEntry {
    mode: string
    type: 'tree' | 'blob' | 'commit'
    sha: string
    /** Bytes, for a blob; null for a tree or a submodule. */
    size: number | null
  }

  export interface PlannedChange {
    path: string
    status: ChangeStatus
    /** The mode written — or, for a deletion, the mode removed. */
    mode: string
    /** A write's exact bytes. */
    bytes?: Buffer
  }

  function kind(e: BaseEntry): string {
    if (e.type === 'tree') return 'a directory'
    if (e.type === 'commit') return 'a submodule'
    return e.mode === '120000' ? 'a symlink' : 'a file'
  }

  function ancestors(path: string): string[] {
    const parts = path.split('/')
    return parts.slice(0, -1).map((_, i) => parts.slice(0, i + 1).join('/'))
  }

  const conflict = (message: string): never => {
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT', message)
  }

  /**
   * THE CONTROL (Decision 2). `update-index --index-info` REPLACES where a person would expect a
   * refusal — a file named `src` deleted the directory `src/`, and a write under a symlink turned
   * the symlink into a directory (measured, *Read this first* 3) — so every such shape is refused
   * HERE, against the base's own listing, before the index is touched. Evaluated against the BASE
   * only: deleting `out` and writing `out/x` in one commit is refused, and two commits do it.
   */
  export function planChanges(
    base: ReadonlyMap<string, BaseEntry>,
    changes: readonly Change[],
  ): PlannedChange[] {
    const named = new Set<string>()
    // The callback's return type is DECLARED: inferred, `status` widens to `string`, and `tsc`
    // refuses `string[]` where `ChangeStatus` belongs — a Vitest run would not notice.
    return changes.map((change): PlannedChange => {
      if (named.has(change.path)) conflict(`'${change.path}' is named twice in one commit`)
      named.add(change.path)
      const here = base.get(change.path)
      if (change.op === 'delete') {
        if (here === undefined) {
          throw new SourceError(
            'SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND',
            `'${change.path}' is not in the base commit, so there is nothing to delete`,
          )
        }
        if (here.type !== 'blob') {
          conflict(`'${change.path}' is ${kind(here)}; Manifest deletes files, one path at a time`)
        }
        return { path: change.path, status: 'deleted', mode: here.mode }
      }
      for (const a of ancestors(change.path)) {
        const e = base.get(a)
        if (e !== undefined && e.type !== 'tree') {
          conflict(`'${change.path}' cannot be written: '${a}' is ${kind(e)} in the base commit, not a directory`)
        }
      }
      if (here !== undefined && !(here.type === 'blob' && REGULAR.has(here.mode))) {
        conflict(`'${change.path}' is ${kind(here)} in the base commit; Manifest writes regular text files only`)
      }
      return {
        path: change.path,
        status: here === undefined ? 'added' : 'modified',
        mode: here?.mode ?? '100644',
        bytes: Buffer.from(change.content, 'utf8'),
      }
    })
  }

  /**
   * git with NOTHING inherited but PATH: an inherited `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`,
   * `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` or `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` would point this at another repository or
   * configure it (the D5 plan's review, minor 5, for the same reason). A failure's message names
   * the verb and git's last words, with the scratch path replaced — the message goes on the wire.
   */
  export function runGit(
    gitDir: string,
    args: readonly string[],
    o: { input?: string | Buffer; env?: Record<string, string>; scratch?: string } = {},
  ): Promise<{ stdout: string; code: number; stderr: string }> {
    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      const child = spawn('git', ['--git-dir', gitDir, ...args], {
        env: {
          PATH: process.env.PATH ?? '/usr/bin:/bin',
          GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: '1',
          GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: '/dev/null',
          GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: '0',
          LC_ALL: 'C',
          ...o.env,
        },
        stdio: ['pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
      })
      const out: Buffer[] = []
      let err = ''
      child.stdout.on('data', (b: Buffer) => out.push(b))
      child.stderr.on('data', (b: Buffer) => (err += b.toString()))
      child.on('error', reject)
      child.on('close', (code) =>
        resolve({ stdout: Buffer.concat(out).toString('utf8'), code: code ?? 1, stderr: err }),
      )
      child.stdin.end(o.input ?? '')
    })
  }

  async function must(
    gitDir: string,
    args: readonly string[],
    o: Parameters<typeof runGit>[2] = {},
  ): Promise<string> {
    const r = await runGit(gitDir, args, o)
    if (r.code === 0) return r.stdout
    const said = r.stderr.trim().split('\n').slice(-2).join(' | ')
    const clean = o.scratch === undefined ? said : said.split(o.scratch).join('<scratch>')
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_GIT_FAILED', `git ${args[0]} failed (${r.code}): ${clean}`)
  }

  /** Every entry of a commit's tree, trees included — what the planner reads. */
  export async function listBase(gitDir: string, commit: string): Promise<Map<string, BaseEntry>> {
    const out = await must(gitDir, ['ls-tree', '-r', '-t', '-l', '-z', '--full-tree', commit])
    const map = new Map<string, BaseEntry>()
    for (const rec of out.split('\0')) {
      if (rec === '') continue
      const tab = rec.indexOf('\t')
      const [mode, type, sha, size] = rec.slice(0, tab).split(/ +/) as [string, BaseEntry['type'], string, string]
      map.set(rec.slice(tab + 1), { mode, type, sha, size: size === '-' ? null : Number(size) })
    }
    return map
  }

  export interface BuiltCommit {
    /** The scratch repository that holds the new objects — push FROM here. */
    gitDir: string
    commit: string
    parent: string | null
    changes: { path: string; status: ChangeStatus }[]
    dispose(): Promise<void>
  }

  /**
   * THE ONE WRITE PATH (Decision 1): no worktree, so no file of the repository is ever written at
   * its own path — nothing for a symlink to redirect, no `.git/config` to replace, no `fsmonitor`,
   * no filter, no hook. `objects` is the git dir whose objects hold `base` (driver 1's bare
   * repository, driver 2's mirror); the scratch repository BORROWS them through `alternates`, so
   * nothing is copied. The caller pushes `commit` from `gitDir`, non-forced, and disposes.
   */
  export async function buildCommit(input: {
    objects: string
    base: string | null
    changes: readonly Change[]
    message: string
    author: GitIdentity
  }): Promise<BuiltCommit> {
    const scratch = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'manifest-commit-'))
    const dispose = () => rm(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true })
    try {
      const gitDir = join(scratch, 'r.git')
      await must(gitDir, ['init', '-q', '--bare', gitDir], { scratch })
      await writeFile(join(gitDir, 'objects', 'info', 'alternates'), `${join(input.objects, 'objects')}\n`)
      const index = { GIT_INDEX_FILE: join(scratch, 'index') }
      const base = input.base === null ? new Map<string, BaseEntry>() : await listBase(gitDir, input.base)
      const plan = planChanges(base, input.changes)
      // Every blob in ONE process, from NUMBERED files — never at the file's own path.
      const writes = plan.filter((c) => c.bytes !== undefined)
      const shas = new Map<string, string>()
      if (writes.length > 0) {
        await mkdir(join(scratch, 'blobs'))
        const files = await Promise.all(
          writes.map(async (c, i) => {
            const f = join(scratch, 'blobs', String(i))
            await writeFile(f, c.bytes!)
            return f
          }),
        )
        const out = (
          await must(gitDir, ['hash-object', '-w', '--no-filters', '--stdin-paths'], {
            input: files.join('\n') + '\n',
            scratch,
          })
        ).trim().split('\n')
        writes.forEach((c, i) => shas.set(c.path, out[i]!))
      }
      // A write that changes nothing is not a change (Decision 2).
      const effective = plan.filter((c) => {
        if (c.status !== 'modified') return true
        const was = base.get(c.path)!
        return !(was.sha === shas.get(c.path) && was.mode === c.mode)
      })
      if (effective.length === 0) {
        throw new SourceError(
          'SOURCE_NOTHING_TO_COMMIT',
          'these changes leave every file as it was in the base commit, so there is nothing to commit',
        )
      }
      if (input.base !== null) await must(gitDir, ['read-tree', input.base], { env: index, scratch })
      const lines = effective
        .map((c) =>
          c.status === 'deleted' ? `0 ${ZERO}\t${c.path}\0` : `${c.mode} ${shas.get(c.path)!}\t${c.path}\0`,
        )
        .join('')
      await must(gitDir, ['update-index', '-z', '--index-info'], { input: lines, env: index, scratch })
      const tree = (await must(gitDir, ['write-tree'], { env: index, scratch })).trim()
      await assertTreeIs(gitDir, tree, base, effective, shas, scratch)
      const commit = (
        await must(
          gitDir,
          ['commit-tree', tree, ...(input.base === null ? [] : ['-p', input.base]), '-F', '-'],
          {
            input: input.message,
            env: {
              GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: input.author.name,
              GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: input.author.email,
              GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: MANIFEST_COMMITTER.name,
              GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: MANIFEST_COMMITTER.email,
            },
            scratch,
          },
        )
      ).trim()
      return {
        gitDir,
        commit,
        parent: input.base,
        changes: effective.map(({ path, status }) => ({ path, status })),
        dispose,
      }
    } catch (error) {
      await dispose()
      throw error
    }
  }

  /**
   * BELT AND BRACES (Decision 2): the tree written must be the base's files, minus the deletions,
   * plus the writes — by path, mode and blob. A planner defect becomes a refusal, never a silent
   * deletion of somebody's directory.
   */
  async function assertTreeIs(
    gitDir: string,
    tree: string,
    base: ReadonlyMap<string, BaseEntry>,
    changes: readonly PlannedChange[],
    shas: ReadonlyMap<string, string>,
    scratch: string,
  ): Promise<void> {
    const expected = new Map<string, string>()
    for (const [p, e] of base) if (e.type !== 'tree') expected.set(p, `${e.mode} ${e.sha}`)
    for (const c of changes) {
      if (c.status === 'deleted') expected.delete(c.path)
      else expected.set(c.path, `${c.mode} ${shas.get(c.path)!}`)
    }
    const actual = new Map<string, string>()
    for (const [p, e] of await listBase(gitDir, tree)) if (e.type !== 'tree') actual.set(p, `${e.mode} ${e.sha}`)
    const wrong = [...new Set([...expected.keys(), ...actual.keys()])].filter((p) => expected.get(p) !== actual.get(p))
    if (wrong.length === 0) return
    console.error(`[source] a planned commit's tree differs from its plan at ${wrong.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}; nothing was committed`)
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_GIT_FAILED', 'the tree written did not match the changes asked for, so nothing was committed')
  }
  ```
  Two notes, both measured while this plan was written (git 2.50.1). **`init`** must name the directory it creates as its own `--git-dir`: write it `must(gitDir, ['init', '-q', '--bare', gitDir], { scratch })` — `git --git-dir=D init --bare D` created `D` (exit 0). **`hash-object -w --no-filters --stdin-paths`** over numbered files answered, in order, exactly the ids `hash-object --no-filters <file>` gives each file alone (a CRLF file and a two-byte UTF-8 file). `listBase(gitDir, …)` inside `buildCommit` reads the base's objects through `alternates`, so no second git dir is needed. **The runner is exported as `runGit`** because driver 1's push in Step 4 uses it; it ANSWERS an exit code rather than throwing, and `must` is the throwing form.
