- [ ] **Step 4: `SourceDriver.commit` on driver 1, and the seed through the same path.** In `local-driver.ts`, **delete `commitThroughWorktree`** and write:
  ```ts
  /**
   * Pushes a built commit into the bare repository, NON-FORCED, so its `pre-receive` runs (the D5
   * plan's Task 11) and a moved `main` is refused by git itself (Decision 3). `--porcelain` puts
   * the verdict on STDOUT — measured while this plan was written: a refusal is exit 1 with
   * `!\t<sha>:refs/heads/main\t[rejected] (non-fast-forward)` on stdout, and only `failed to push`
   * and hints on stderr — so it is stdout that is read.
   */
  async function pushInto(bare: string, built: BuiltCommit): Promise<void> {
    const r = await runGit(built.gitDir, ['push', '--porcelain', bare, `${built.commit}:refs/heads/main`])
    if (r.code === 0) return
    if (/\[rejected\] \((non-fast-forward|fetch first)\)/.test(r.stdout)) {
      throw new SourceError('SOURCE_CONFLICT', 'main moved while this commit was being made; read the tree again and retry')
    }
    // `[remote rejected] (pre-receive hook declined)`: the hook is the backstop behind the
    // pre-push scan, and reaching it means the two disagree — an operator's problem, said once.
    console.error(`[source] the repository refused Manifest's own push: ${`${r.stdout}\n${r.stderr}`.trim().split('\n').slice(-4).join(' | ')}`)
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_GIT_FAILED', 'the repository refused the push; nothing was committed')
  }
  ```
  **A push git refused must never read as success**: `runGit` answers the exit code rather than throwing, and `pushInto` returns only on `0` — Step 8's control (c) is that assertion. Then `commit`:
  ```ts
  async commit(repo, request) {
    const path = assertOwned(repo)
    assertWritablePaths(request.changes.map((c) => c.path))
    assertNoSecrets(writesOf(request.changes))
    const head = (await git(path, ['rev-parse', '--verify', 'refs/heads/main^{commit}'])).trim()
    if (request.base !== head) {
      throw new SourceError('SOURCE_CONFLICT', `main is ${head.slice(0, 12)} now, and these changes were computed from ${request.base.slice(0, 12)}; read the tree again and retry`)
    }
    // THE CLIENT'S BASE, never the head read above: were the check above ever lost, a commit
    // built on the head would replay stale changes over a person's push; built on the base, git
    // refuses it non-fast-forward at the push — two layers, each able to fail alone (Step 8 (c)).
    const built = await buildCommit({ objects: path, base: request.base, changes: request.changes, message: request.message, author: request.author })
    try {
      if (request.dryRun !== true) await pushInto(path, built)
      return { commitSha: request.dryRun === true ? null : built.commit, parent: request.base, changes: built.changes }
    } finally {
      await built.dispose()
    }
  },
  ```
  with `writesOf = (cs: readonly Change[]) => cs.flatMap((c) => (c.op === 'write' ? [{ path: c.path, content: c.content }] : []))`, exported from `scan-commits.ts` beside the two assertions. `createRepository` keeps `assertWritablePaths(Object.keys(seed))` and `assertNoSecrets(...)` and replaces `commitThroughWorktree(path, seed, …, true)` with `buildCommit({ objects: path, base: null, changes: Object.entries(seed).map(([p, content]) => ({ op: 'write', path: p, content })), message: 'chore: seed from blueprint skeleton', author: MANIFEST_COMMITTER })` then `pushInto(path, built)` then `dispose()`. **`GIT_IDENTITY` goes with `commitThroughWorktree`** unless another reader remains (`grep` it).

## Task 4: The read primitives — a tree, a text file, a history and one commit's changes, on both drivers

> **`[S2]` — WHAT TASK 3 BUILT, WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM THE TEXT BELOW (2026-09-25, sitting 2; its record).**
> `source/plumbing.ts` exports `planChanges`, `buildCommit`, `listBase`, `runGit`, `pushVerdict` and `MANIFEST_COMMITTER`.
> **`runGit(gitDir, args, { input?, env? })`** answers `{ stdout, code, stderr }` and never throws; the scratch-path
> scrubbing is the private `must`'s, not `runGit`'s. **`assertWritablePaths(paths)` also refuses a path that is not inside
> the repository** (a leading `/`, an empty, `.` or `..` component, a NUL) as `SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE` (F9), and
> **`assertNoSecrets` takes `{ path, content }[]`**, with `writesOf(changes)` beside it in `scan-commits.ts`. **Tests commit
> through `source/testing.ts`'s `writeFiles(driver, repo, files, message)`**, authored `TEST_AUTHOR`, and both harnesses
> have `pushSymlinkAsPerson(slug, path, target, message)`. **A refused push reads the BRANCH on both drivers** — a race lost
> inside git's receive-pack is `SOURCE_CONFLICT` too (F11). And the record's F14: with the planner AND the tree check
> broken, a write can delete what it replaces and vanish itself — a read primitive that trusts a tree it did not list is
> the same class of mistake.

> **`[M10]` — MEASURED (2026-09-25, sitting 1, F13): THE CAPS STAND.** On a bare repository of 10,001 files (10,000 of ~30
> bytes across 200 directories, and one of 20 MB), `git ls-tree -r -t -l -z --full-tree HEAD` takes 0.02 s and writes
> 763,670 bytes, and `git diff --numstat <empty tree> HEAD` 0.05–0.06 s (10,001 lines), at load ~3.8. So a listing AT the
> 10,000-entry cap is fast, and its answer is of the order of a megabyte of JSON: keep the cap, and do not raise it for
> speed's sake. Nothing measured contradicts the 256 KiB patch cap.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/source/reading.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/source/reading.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/git-driver.ts` — the five read methods and their types
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/local-driver.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/source/github/driver.ts` — each resolves its own git dir, then delegates to `reading.ts`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/driver-contract.ts` — the read cases
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/error-codes.ts` — `SOURCE_PATH_NOT_A_FILE`, `SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT`, `SOURCE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`, `SOURCE_REF_NOT_FOUND`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `listBase`, `runGit` (Task 3's `plumbing.ts`); driver 1's `assertOwned`/`assertCommit`; driver 2's `mirrorOf`/`present`/`sync`/`UPSTREAM`.
- Produces, in `git-driver.ts`:
  ```ts
  export type EntryType = 'file' | 'directory' | 'symlink' | 'submodule'
  export interface SourceEntry { path: string; type: EntryType; mode: string; size: number | null; binary: boolean | null }
  export interface TextFile { path: string; content: string; size: number; mode: string; blobSha: string }
  export interface CommitInfo {
    commitSha: string; parents: string[]; subject: string
    message: string; messageTruncated: boolean
    authorName: string; authoredAt: string   // ISO 8601
  }
  export type FileChangeStatus = 'added' | 'modified' | 'deleted' | 'type_changed'
  export interface FileChange {
    path: string; status: FileChangeStatus; binary: boolean
    additions: number | null; deletions: number | null
    patch: string | null      // null for a binary file, or past the budget
  }
  export interface CommitDetail extends CommitInfo { changes: FileChange[]; patchesTruncated: boolean }
  // SourceDriver gains:
  resolveRef(repo: RepoRef, ref: string): Promise<string>
  listTree(repo: RepoRef, commitSha: string): Promise<{ entries: SourceEntry[]; truncated: boolean }>
  readText(repo: RepoRef, commitSha: string, path: string): Promise<TextFile>
  history(repo: RepoRef, from: string, limit: number): Promise<{ commits: CommitInfo[]; next: string | null }>
  describeCommit(repo: RepoRef, commitSha: string): Promise<CommitDetail>
  ```
  and in `reading.ts` the same five as functions over a git dir: `listTreeIn(gitDir, commit)`, `readTextIn(gitDir, commit, path)`, `historyIn(gitDir, from, limit)`, `describeCommitIn(gitDir, commit)`, plus `READ_LIMITS = { treeEntries: 10_000, fileBytes: 1024 * 1024, messageChars: 4096, patchBytes: 256 * 1024 }`. **Task 5's routes call the driver's five by these names.**

**What each does, exactly** (all read-only, all through `runGit` with nothing inherited):

| Method | git | Answers / refuses |
|---|---|---|
| `resolveRef` | driver 1: a 40-hex id is `assertCommit`ed; a name is `rev-parse --verify refs/heads/<name>^{commit}`. Driver 2: `sync` first, then a name is `${UPSTREAM}/<name>` (GitHub's NOW) and an id is `present` | the commit id; **`SOURCE_REF_NOT_FOUND`** for a branch that does not exist (today `headCommit` says `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED` for that — a missing branch is not a git failure); `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` for an id the repository lacks. A `ref` is `^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,255}$` with no `..` and not starting `-` — checked in the route's query schema, AND again here, because a name that begins `-` reaching git's argv is an option |
| `listTree` | `listBase` (Task 3), and `git diff --numstat -z 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904 <commit>` for git's own binary flags (`-\t-\t<path>`), in one process each | every entry — `040000` a `directory`, `120000` a `symlink`, `160000` a `submodule`, `100644`/`100755` a `file` — sorted by path; `size` for a file or a symlink, else `null`; `binary` for a file, else `null`. **Past `READ_LIMITS.treeEntries`, the first 10,000 by path and `truncated: true`** (Task 1's `[M10]` may move the number) |
| `readText` | `git ls-tree -z --full-tree <commit> -- <path>`, then `git cat-file blob <sha>` as bytes | the file. **`SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND`** when nothing is there; **`SOURCE_PATH_NOT_A_FILE`** for a directory, a symlink or a submodule; **`SOURCE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`** past 1 MiB (read the size from `ls-tree -l` first — never load the blob to refuse it); **`SOURCE_FILE_NOT_TEXT`** for a NUL in the first 8000 bytes or bytes that `new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true })` refuses |
| `history` | `git log -z --format=%H%x1f%P%x1f%an%x1f%aI%x1f%B --max-count=<limit+1> <from>` | newest first; `limit` commits and **`next` = the id of the `limit+1`th, or `null`** — a client passes it back as `cursor` and the next page starts AT it. `subject` is the message's first line; `message` is cut at 4096 characters with `messageTruncated`. **Never the author's email**: git's author address can be a person's own, and the API has no reason to publish it |
| `describeCommit` | its first parent (or the empty tree, for a root commit): `git diff --no-renames -z --name-status` and `--numstat` against it, then **one `git diff --no-renames --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv -U3 <parent> <commit> -- <path>` per text file** until 256 KiB of patch has been read | `changes` in path order; `type_changed` for git's `T`; a binary file's `patch` is `null` and its counts `null`; once the budget is spent every later file's `patch` is `null` and **`patchesTruncated: true`** |

- Produces: the operations **`getTree`** `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/tree?ref=`, **`getFile`** `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/file?path=&ref=`, **`listCommits`** `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/commits?ref=&limit=&cursor=`, **`getCommit`** `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/commits/{commitSha}`, tag **`source`**; the representations **`SourceTree`**, **`SourceFile`**, **`CommitSummary`**, **`CommitList`**, **`CommitDetail`**; and `RouteDefinition.examples?: { request?: unknown; response: unknown }`. **Tasks 6, 9, 10 and 11 use these names.**

  ```ts
  import { z } from 'zod/v4'
  import { representation, Timestamp } from '../contract/schemas.js'

  export const CommitSha = z.string().regex(/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/).describe('A full 40-character commit id.')
  export const Ref = z
    .string()
    .regex(/^(?!-)(?!.*\.\.)[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,255}$/)
    .describe('A branch name, or a full 40-character commit id. Defaults to `main`.')

  const Entry = z.object({
    path: z.string().describe('The path from the repository root, `/`-separated.'),
    type: z.enum(['file', 'directory', 'symlink', 'submodule']).describe('What git records at this path. The API reads and writes `file`s only.'),
    mode: z.string().regex(/^[0-7]{6}$/).describe("git's mode: `100644` a file, `100755` an executable file, `120000` a symlink, `040000` a directory, `160000` a submodule."),
    size: z.number().int().nonnegative().nullable().describe('Bytes, for a file or a symlink; null otherwise.'),
    binary: z.boolean().nullable().describe('Whether git calls this file binary — such a file is not readable or writable through the API in v1. Null for anything that is not a file.'),
  })

  export const SourceTree = representation('SourceTree', z.object({
    ref: Ref,
    commitSha: CommitSha.describe('The commit the ref resolved to — what this listing is OF. Send it as `baseCommit` when committing changes computed from it.'),
    entries: z.array(Entry).describe('Every entry of the tree, sorted by path.'),
    truncated: z.boolean().describe('True when the tree has more than 10,000 entries and only the first 10,000, by path, are listed.'),
  }))

  export const SourceFile = representation('SourceFile', z.object({
    ref: Ref,
    commitSha: CommitSha.describe('The commit the file was read at.'),
    path: z.string().describe('The file’s path from the repository root.'),
    content: z.string().describe('The file’s text, exactly — UTF-8, at most 1 MiB.'),
    size: z.number().int().nonnegative().describe('The file’s size in bytes.'),
    mode: z.string().regex(/^[0-7]{6}$/).describe('`100644`, or `100755` for an executable file — kept when the file is changed.'),
    blobSha: z.string().regex(/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/).describe("git's id for this content; equal ids mean equal bytes."),
  }))

  const summaryShape = {
    commitSha: CommitSha,
    parents: z.array(CommitSha).describe('Its parents, first parent first; empty for the first commit.'),
    subject: z.string().describe('The first line of the commit message.'),
    message: z.string().describe('The whole commit message, cut at 4096 characters.'),
    messageTruncated: z.boolean().describe('True when `message` was cut.'),
    authorName: z.string().describe("The author git recorded. For a commit made through the API this is the person's name; for any other push it is whatever the pusher's git said, and is not verified."),
    authoredAt: Timestamp.describe('When git says the commit was authored.'),
  }
  export const CommitSummary = representation('CommitSummary', z.object(summaryShape))
  export const CommitList = representation('CommitList', z.object({
    ref: Ref,
    commits: z.array(CommitSummary).describe('Newest first.'),
    next: CommitSha.nullable().describe('Pass as `cursor` for the next page; null on the last page.'),
  }))

  const FileChange = z.object({
    path: z.string().describe('The file’s path.'),
    status: z.enum(['added', 'modified', 'deleted', 'type_changed']).describe("What happened to it, against the commit's first parent."),
    binary: z.boolean().describe('Whether git calls the file binary; a binary file has no patch and no line counts.'),
    additions: z.number().int().nonnegative().nullable().describe('Lines added; null for a binary file.'),
    deletions: z.number().int().nonnegative().nullable().describe('Lines removed; null for a binary file.'),
    patch: z.string().nullable().describe('A unified diff with three lines of context; null for a binary file, or once 256 KiB of patch has been given.'),
  })
  export const CommitDetail = representation('CommitDetail', z.object({
    ...summaryShape,
    changes: z.array(FileChange).describe('Every file the commit changed, by path.'),
    patchesTruncated: z.boolean().describe('True when some `patch` is null because the 256 KiB budget was spent.'),
  }))
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: The request and the answer.**
  ```ts
  /** Decision 4's path rules, as data a client can read. */
  export const RepoPath = z
    .string()
    .min(1)
    .superRefine((p, ctx) => {
      const problem = pathProblem(p)
      if (problem !== null) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: problem })
    })
    .describe('A relative path, `/`-separated: at most 1024 bytes, 255 per component and 32 components; no empty, `.` or `..` component, no leading or trailing `/`, no backslash, no control character, and no `.git` component.')

  const WriteChange = z.strictObject({
    op: z.literal('write').describe('Create the file, or replace its content.'),
    path: RepoPath,
    content: z
      .string()
      .superRefine((c, ctx) => {
        if (!c.isWellFormed()) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: 'content is not well-formed Unicode (a lone surrogate)' })
        if (c.includes('\u0000')) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: 'content contains a NUL character; the API writes text files only' })
        if (Buffer.byteLength(c, 'utf8') > 1024 * 1024) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: 'content is larger than 1 MiB of UTF-8' })
      })
      .describe('The whole new content of the file, as text: at most 1 MiB of UTF-8, with no NUL character.'),
  })
  const DeleteChange = z.strictObject({
    op: z.literal('delete').describe('Remove the file. It must exist in `baseCommit`.'),
    path: RepoPath,
  })

  export const CreateCommitRequest = request('CreateCommitRequest', z.strictObject({
    baseCommit: CommitSha.describe('The commit these changes were computed from — `commitSha` from the tree or file you read. `main` must still be exactly this commit, or the request is refused `SOURCE_CONFLICT`.'),
    message: z.string().min(1).max(4096).describe('The commit message. Its first line is its subject.'),
    changes: z
      .array(z.discriminatedUnion('op', [WriteChange, DeleteChange]))
      .min(1)
      .max(500)
      .describe('At most 500 writes and deletions, each naming a different path.'),
    dryRun: z.boolean().optional().describe('Run every check the commit would, write nothing, and answer what would have happened.'),
  }))

  export const CommitOutcome = representation('CommitOutcome', z.object({
    dryRun: z.boolean().describe('True when nothing was written.'),
    commitSha: CommitSha.nullable().describe('The new commit on `main`; null for a dry run.'),
    parent: CommitSha.describe('The commit this one follows — the request’s `baseCommit`.'),
    changes: z.array(z.object({
      path: z.string().describe('The file’s path.'),
      status: z.enum(['added', 'modified', 'deleted']).describe('What the commit did to it.'),
    })).describe('What changed. A write that left a file as it was is not listed.'),
    spec: z.object({
      appSpecId: Uuid.nullable().describe('The recorded validation of the new commit; null for a dry run.'),
      sensitiveDiff: SensitiveDiff,
    }).describe("The new commit's manifest.yaml — always valid, because an invalid one is refused `SPEC_INVALID` before anything is written."),
  }))
  ```
  `pathProblem` lives in `source/plumbing.ts` beside the planner, so the route's schema and any later caller state the rules once:
  ```ts
  /** Decision 4's path rules, in the order a person would fix them; null when the path is acceptable. */
  export function pathProblem(path: string): string | null {
    if (Buffer.byteLength(path, 'utf8') > 1024) return 'a path is at most 1024 bytes'
    if (path.startsWith('/') || path.endsWith('/')) return 'a path is relative, with no leading or trailing /'
    if (/[\u0000-\u001f\u007f\\]/.test(path)) return 'a path has no control character and no backslash'
    const parts = path.split('/')
    if (parts.length > 32) return 'a path is at most 32 components deep'
    for (const part of parts) {
      if (part === '' || part === '.' || part === '..') return 'a path has no empty, . or .. component'
      if (Buffer.byteLength(part, 'utf8') > 255) return 'a path component is at most 255 bytes'
      if (part.toLowerCase() === '.git') return "a path never names a repository's own .git"
    }
    return null
  }
  ```
  with its own cases in `plumbing.test.ts` — one per rule, and a positive control (`src/a b/é.js` is acceptable); the `.git`-component rule is **also** `assertWritablePaths`' `SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE` in the drivers — **the schema refuses it first as `REQUEST_INVALID`**, and the driver's refusal stays as the second read (the D5 plan's review, Important 1). **Check `String.prototype.isWellFormed` is in Node 24's lib types** (`@types/node` / `lib: ES2024`); if `tsc` refuses it, the compiled answer is a one-line loop over surrogate pairs — say which in the commit.

  ```ts
  defineRoute({
    operationId: 'createCommit',
    method: 'POST',
    path: '/v1/projects/{projectId}/commits',
    tag: 'source',
    summary: 'Commit changes to main',
    description:
      'Writes and deletes text files on the project’s `main`, as one commit computed from `baseCommit`. Every change is checked before anything is written — the paths, the text, secret-shaped values, the base, the tree, and the manifest.yaml the commit would leave, which must be valid. `dryRun: true` runs every check and writes nothing. A retry with the same Idempotency-Key answers the first commit again.',
    params: ProjectParams,
    query: NO_QUERY,
    body: CreateCommitRequest,
    bodyLimit: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
    success: { status: 201, description: 'The commit, or what it would have been.', schema: CommitOutcome },
    errors: [
      'NOT_FOUND', 'FORBIDDEN', 'SPEC_INVALID',
      'SOURCE_CONFLICT', 'SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT', 'SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND', 'SOURCE_NOTHING_TO_COMMIT',
      'SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE', 'SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED', 'SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH', 'SOURCE_UNREACHABLE',
      'SOURCE_GIT_FAILED', 'AI_BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE', 'AI_CATALOGUE_EMPTY',
    ],
    examples: { request: /* Step 2's first payload */, response: /* its answer, captured */ },
    handler: async ({ deps, actor, params, body }) => {
      await assertCapability(deps.db, actor, params.projectId, 'source:write')
      const project = await getProject(deps.db, params.projectId)
      if (project === undefined) throw new AuthorizationError('NOT_FOUND', `no project '${params.projectId}'`)
      const repo = await repositoryOf(deps, project)
      const dryRun = body.dryRun === true
      // 1. Secrets — found HERE, as data, so the refusal can be published without the value.
      const findings = secretFindings(writesOf(body.changes))
      if (findings.length > 0) {
        if (!dryRun) await publishSecretRefused(deps, project, findings)
        throw secretRefusal(findings) // the SourceError assertNoSecrets throws, built from the same findings
      }
      // 2. The base, the planner and the tree — the driver's dry run (Decision 6's order).
      const author = await authorFor(deps.db, actor)
      const request = { base: body.baseCommit, changes: body.changes, message: body.message, author }
      const planned = await deps.source.commit(repo, { ...request, dryRun: true })
      // 3. The manifest the commit would leave, validated with validateAndRecord's own function.
      const yamlText = await manifestAfter(deps, repo, body.baseCommit, body.changes)
      const verdict = await validateManifestText(deps, project, yamlText)
      if (!verdict.valid) throw new SpecInvalidError(verdict.errors as never)
      const sensitiveDiff = await sensitiveAgainstNewestValid(deps, project.id, verdict.spec)
      if (dryRun) {
        return { dryRun: true, commitSha: null, parent: planned.parent, changes: planned.changes, spec: { appSpecId: null, sensitiveDiff } }
      }
      // 4. The commit — the driver checks the base again, and git refuses a race at the push.
      const done = await deps.source.commit(repo, request)
      const recorded = await validateAndRecord(deps, project, done.commitSha!)
      await publishCommitted(deps, project, actor, done)
      return { dryRun: false, commitSha: done.commitSha, parent: done.parent, changes: done.changes, spec: { appSpecId: recorded.appSpecId, sensitiveDiff: recorded.sensitiveDiff } }
    },
  })
  ```
  **Four helpers are this step's to write, each small and each tested through the route**: `manifestAfter` — the write's content if the commit writes `manifest.yaml`, `''` if it deletes it (which validates as invalid, so deleting the manifest is refused `SPEC_INVALID`), else `deps.source.readText(repo, base, 'manifest.yaml').content`; `sensitiveAgainstNewestValid` — `isSensitiveDiff` against the newest VALID `app_specs` row, exactly as `validateAndRecord` computes it (**extract the one query both use; never a second copy**); `publishCommitted` — `repository.committed` with the counts from `done.changes`, `via` from `actor.credential`, `tokenId` for a token, and the sentence *"<Name> committed <n> change(s) to main: <subject, cut at 72>"* — for a token *"<Name>'s agent (token '<token name>') committed …"*; and `publishSecretRefused`. **`authorFor`** (`projects/source-attribution.ts`) reads `users.display_name` as `releases/approval.ts:86-90` does (the PUID only when the name is empty), and for a token also `delegated_tokens.name`; its email is `${userId}@users.manifest.internal`. **The planner runs twice on a real commit** (the dry run, then the commit) — accepted: it is a tree listing and a hash, and a commit that could not be planned is refused before the manifest is read. Record the time it adds in Step 7.

> **`[S4]` — WHAT TASK 6 MET THAT THIS TASK WILL MEET (2026-09-26, sitting 4; its record).** Five things this task's Files
> list does not name, each of which turned a gate red for Task 6:
> 1. **`String.prototype.isWellFormed` is not in `tsc`'s lib** — `tsconfig.base.json` targets ES2023 and it is ES2024 —
>    so Step 1's `v.isWellFormed()` is a type error. Task 6 used `/\p{Surrogate}/u` (`api/representations/source.ts`'s
>    `LONE_SURROGATE`, measured equal to `isWellFormed` on a lone high, a lone low, a pair and `é`); reuse it.
> 2. **A capability added to `CAPABILITIES` is a `tsc` error in the CONSOLE** until `packages/console/src/screens/tokens.tsx`'s
>    `everyCapability([...])` lists it — which is what that function is for. `secret:write` goes there, and in the
>    authorization matrix's `token-capable` list (`api/authz-contract.ts`, `CAPABLE`) if the route is to pass on a token.
> 3. **A new event type must be REACHED by `api/stream-contract.test.ts`'s lifecycle** or listed in its
>    `PUBLISHED_ELSEWHERE` — Task 6's first full run was red there. Task 6 DROVE its two types in the lifecycle (a commit, a
>    refused secret), so both frames are parsed; do the same with a set and a cleared secret.
> 4. **The migration must be APPLIED to `manifest_control` before the unit tier runs** — the harness TRUNCATEs the
>    platform's own database and never migrates it, so a new `CHECK` literal is a `500` on every insert until
>    `db:migrate` runs (ORIENTATION §3 has the one-line form). Task 6 saw it as ten reds of `500 INTERNAL`.
> 5. **`RouteCase.request` may be async since Task 6** (`Fixture.mainHead`), and `createCommit`'s row shows a mutating
>    row that must not depend on another actor's run. Its `[S3]` block's two package gates still apply.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `putSecret`, `getSecret`, `deleteSecret`, `secretValuesFor` (`secrets/store.ts`); `resolveConfig`; `RESERVED_ENV_NAMES` (`spec/injection.ts:140`); `requireSession`, `assertStepUp`.
- Produces: operations **`listAppSecrets`** `GET /v1/environments/{environmentId}/secrets`, **`setAppSecret`** `PUT /v1/environments/{environmentId}/secrets/{name}` (the API's first `PUT`), **`clearAppSecret`** `DELETE /v1/environments/{environmentId}/secrets/{name}`; representations **`AppSecretList`**, **`AppSecretStatus`**, **`SetAppSecretRequest`**; the capability **`secret:write`**. **Tasks 10, 11 and 13 use these names.**

- [ ] **Step 1: The representations.**
  ```ts
  const SecretName = z.string().regex(/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,127}$/).describe('The variable’s name, as manifest.yaml’s `env` declares it.')
  export const AppSecretStatus = representation('AppSecretStatus', z.object({
    name: SecretName,
    declared: z.boolean().describe('Whether the environment’s newest valid manifest.yaml declares this name with `secret: true`. Only a declared name reaches the app.'),
    set: z.boolean().describe('Whether a value is stored. The value itself is never answered by any operation.'),
    updatedAt: Timestamp.nullable().describe('When the value was last set; null when none is.'),
  }))
  export const AppSecretList = representation('AppSecretList', z.object({
    environmentId: Uuid,
    environmentKind: z.enum(['sandbox', 'staging', 'production']),
    secrets: z.array(AppSecretStatus).describe('Every name declared or set, sorted. A declared name with `set: false` stops the next deploy (`RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET`).'),
  }))
  export const SetAppSecretRequest = request('SetAppSecretRequest', z.strictObject({
    value: z.string().min(1).superRefine((v, ctx) => {
      if (!v.isWellFormed() || v.includes('\u0000')) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: 'a value is text: well-formed, with no NUL character' })
      if (Buffer.byteLength(v, 'utf8') > 16384) ctx.addIssue({ code: 'custom', message: 'a value is at most 16384 bytes of UTF-8' })
    }).describe('The value, as text. Takes effect at the next deploy of this environment; it is never answered back.'),
  }))
  ```

- [ ] **Step 2: The failing tests** (`api/secrets.test.ts`).

  it('a person sets a production value only with step-up', async () => { /* ownerCookies → 403 STEP_UP_REQUIRED; ownerSteppedUp → 200 */ })

  ```ts

  it('renders a set value into the container, and redacts it from an Incident', async () => { /* set it; deploy an app that prints the variable and exits 1 → the Incident's log_tail holds [REDACTED] where the value would be, and never the value; a healthy app's container env (docker inspect) has it */ })
  ```
  Run — **Expected: FAIL** (`404 ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`).

- [ ] **Step 3: `secrets/app-env.ts`.** Values are stored as **`env:<NAME>`** in `secrets`, scoped to the project and the environment KIND (the store's own scope). `setAppSecret(db, keys, scope, name, value)` is `putSecret`, and answers the row's `rotatedAt ?? createdAt` — **read `putSecret`: if it does not set `rotated_at` on an update, `updatedAt` would never move; make it, and test it**. `clearAppSecret` is `deleteSecret` (idempotent). `appSecretStatuses(db, scope, declared: string[])` joins the declared names with the stored `env:` names, sorted. `appEnvSecretValues(db, keys, scope, names)` answers a `Map` of the declared names that have values. **Declared names come from the newest VALID `app_specs` row, resolved for the environment's kind** — `resolveConfig(spec, kind, defaults).env.filter((e) => e.secret === true).map((e) => e.name)` — so an `environments.production.env` override that declares a secret is seen for production only.

- [ ] **Step 4: The deploy.** In `releases/release.ts`, immediately after the digest check (`RELEASE_DIGEST_NOT_APPROVED`) and before `assertPromotable`: read the release's frozen config for this environment, take its `secret: true` names, read their values with `deps.appSecrets.envSecrets`, and **refuse `RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET` naming every missing one** (`ReleaseError`, a literal). The values go to `renderInjection` through `InjectionContext.secrets.appEnv`, and `spec/injection.ts` renders them in the app's own section — beside `resolved.env`'s values, **before** the platform's rows, which still overwrite (`RESERVED_ENV_NAMES` keeps a declared name from colliding at validation). **The rehearsal inherits the refusal**: it deploys through `deployRelease` (ORIENTATION §3).

- [ ] **Step 5: The routes, the capability, the events.** Each route finds the environment's project FROM THE ENVIRONMENT ROW (`getEnvironment`'s pattern — the IDOR lesson of P2), then `assertCapability(…, 'secret:write')` (or `'project:read'` for the list). **For production** — with Spec action 2 approved — `requireSession(request)` then `assertStepUp(actor, 'secret:write')`, and `'secret:write'` joins `STEP_UP_GUARDED`; with it declined, neither, and the production rows in the matrix read like staging's. `app_secret.set` / `app_secret.cleared` `{ environmentKind, name }`, the sentence naming the person (Task 6's `authorFor` has the name). **Never the value in a row, an event, a log line or an error.**

- [ ] **Step 6: The gates; commit.** The matrix's rows; `pnpm contract:write && pnpm contract:generate`; the four gates; `pnpm test` twice; **`pnpm test:docker` in the background** (`secrets/`, `releases/`, `spec/`, a new `*.docker.test.ts`). Commit:
  ```bash
  git commit -m "feat(secrets): an app's declared secrets are set write-only per environment, rendered at deploy, and refused when missing"
  ```

- [ ] **Step 7: Watch it fail.** (a) Restore injection's `filter((e) => e.value !== undefined)` without the new section — predict the Docker case *renders a set value* red (the variable absent from `docker inspect`), and **no unit test red** — record that only the Docker tier sees it. (b) Remove the `RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET` check — predict the Docker case red on an instance row existing. (c) `listAppSecrets` maps the stored value into `name` by mistake (`.map(([name, value]) => value)` — the `.map` index trap's cousin) — predict *no operation ever answers the value* red on `not.toContain('swordfish')`.

## Task 9: The reference, completed — every operation, parameter, schema, property, error code and event documented, an example on every operation, and a gate that keeps it so

> **`[M6]`, `[M7]` AND `[M8]` — MEASURED (2026-09-25, sitting 1; F4, F5, F6, F7, F11; `spikes/authoring-baseline/README.md`).**
>
> **`[M6]`**: every predicted count held (`1.2.0`, 43 / 64 / 786 / 556 / 0 / 99); **35 lines** of public text name an
> internal artefact; 34 schemas have no description; and **four** operation descriptions are fragments — `getBlueprint`
> (30 characters) as well as the three at 28.
>
> **`[M7]` — Ruling, by the decision rule's own terms: `zod-to-json-schema` 3.25.2, and NO port to zod/v4.** The `env`
> entry's `.refine` (`value` XOR `secret: true`) is dropped SILENTLY by `zod-to-json-schema` and ALSO by zod/v4's
> `z.toJSONSchema`, whose `unrepresentable: 'throw'` does not throw for a refine — so porting buys nothing. Everything else
> an agent writes is represented (`runtime.build` as `{"not":{}}`, the hooks `maxItems: 0`, `additionalProperties: false`,
> 23 defaults). So: **(1) restate the XOR by hand** on the `env` item of the emitted schema; **(2) hold the emitted schema to
> zod with a corpus gate** — every starter's `manifest.yaml`, the skeleton's seed and a mutation per rule, each through
> `manifestSchema.safeParse` AND Ajv 2020 over the emitted schema, **refusing any disagreement** (measured: 7 of 9 agree
> before the restatement, and the two refine cases are the ones it must close); **(3) there is no 2020-12 target**
> (`jsonSchema7`, `jsonSchema2019-09`, `openApi3`, `openAi`) — emit `jsonSchema7` with `$refStrategy: 'none'` and drop its
> `$schema` when embedding it in the 3.1 document; the schema uses no construct that differs; **(4) add it to
> `packages/control-plane`**, which already depends on `zod@3.25.76`, with `pnpm add --offline zod-to-json-schema@3.25.2`,
> and **read `pnpm-lock.yaml`'s peer suffix — it must link `zod@3.25.76`**. In an empty package its peer auto-installs as
> `zod@4.6.5`, and sitting 1's Scalar fetch put `zod@4.6.5` in the store, so an offline add now succeeds either way and
> could link the wrong zod without a word.
>
> **`[M8]`**: `@redocly/openapi-core` 1.34.20 installs offline (20 reused, 0 downloaded); the API is `createConfig({
> extends: ['recommended'] })` and `lintFromString({ source, absoluteRef, config })`. **53 warnings, 0 errors, 4 rules, 0
> network attempts** (a canary with a positive control). Each needs a decision in `lint.test.ts`, none silent:
> **`operation-4xx-response` ×42** — every route's errors are ONE `default` response whose description lists its codes

>    (*Global Constraints*), so the two cannot both hold as written (F22).** A red `docs.test.ts` in the tree makes every

>    required `examples` and the linter land LAST, in one commit, green** — and `ALLOWED_GAPS` is empty in every commit
>    it appears in.

**The largest writing task in the plan, and the one Rich called vital.** Its product is prose, so its definition of done is a GATE (Decision 14), written first and red, then brought to green module by module. **The gate's allowlist starts empty and ends empty.**

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/docs.test.ts` — the completeness gate
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/lint.test.ts` — `@redocly/openapi-core` over `openapi.json`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/route.ts` — `examples` REQUIRED (`examples: { request?: z.input<B>; response: z.input<R> }`)
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/document.ts` — `x-enumDescriptions` on `ErrorCode`, the `x-manifest-errors` table, the events on the stream's path item, the `ManifestYaml` component
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/websocket.ts` — `x-manifest-event-types`: each type, its description and its example
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/error-codes.ts` — `remedy` on every code
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/observability/event-schemas.ts` — a description on every event type and field
- Modify: **every** `packages/control-plane/src/api/routes/*.ts` (examples; descriptions that meet the gate) and **every** `packages/control-plane/src/api/representations/*.ts` (`.describe()` on every field), and `packages/control-plane/src/api/contract/schemas.ts`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/package.json` — `zod-to-json-schema@3.25.2` and `@redocly/openapi-core@1.34.20` (**`pnpm add --offline`**, Task 1's `[M7]`/`[M8]`); `pnpm-lock.yaml`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `openApiDocument(ROUTE_DEFINITIONS)`, `ERROR_CODES`, `EVENT_DETAIL_SCHEMAS`, `EXAMPLE_DETAILS`, `manifestSchema`.
- Produces: a document in which every operation has `description` and examples; every parameter, component and property a `description`; `components.schemas.ErrorCode['x-enumDescriptions']`; top-level `x-manifest-errors: { [code]: { status, summary, remedy } }`; the stream path item's `x-manifest-event-types: [{ type, description, example }]`; `components.schemas.ManifestYaml`. **Task 10's mock reads the examples; Task 11's generator reads all of it.**

- [ ] **Step 1: The gate, first, and red.**
  ```ts
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
  import { openApiDocument } from './document.js'
  import { ROUTE_DEFINITIONS } from '../routes/index.js'
  import { ERROR_CODES } from '../error-codes.js'

  type Json = Record<string, unknown>
  const doc = openApiDocument(ROUTE_DEFINITIONS) as Json

  /** Text a reader OUTSIDE the team cannot resolve (Decision 14). The spec's own §n, Dnn and Cn are allowed. */
  const INTERNAL = /\b(P[1-6][abc]?|sitting|Task \d+|Decision \d+|Rich|Rich's)\b|the D5 plan|\bR[1-9]\b|\bF\d{1,3}\b/

  /** EMPTY, and it stays empty. A line here is a finding with a reason, never a way to go green. */
  const ALLOWED_GAPS: readonly string[] = []

  function* properties(schema: unknown, at: string): Generator<[string, Json]> {
    if (schema === null || typeof schema !== 'object') return
    const s = schema as Json
    for (const [name, prop] of Object.entries((s.properties ?? {}) as Record<string, Json>)) {
      yield [`${at}.${name}`, prop]
      yield* properties(prop, `${at}.${name}`)
    }
    for (const key of ['items', 'additionalProperties'] as const) yield* properties(s[key], `${at}[]`)
    for (const key of ['anyOf', 'oneOf', 'allOf'] as const)
      for (const [i, sub] of ((s[key] ?? []) as Json[]).entries()) yield* properties(sub, `${at}|${i}`)
  }

  describe('the published reference is complete (Decision 14)', () => {
    const gaps: string[] = []
    const paths = doc.paths as Record<string, Record<string, Json>>
    for (const [path, item] of Object.entries(paths)) {
      for (const [method, op] of Object.entries(item)) {
        if (typeof op !== 'object' || op === null || !('operationId' in op)) continue
        const id = `${method.toUpperCase()} ${path} (${op.operationId as string})`
        if (typeof op.description !== 'string' || !/[.!?]\s*$/.test(op.description) || op.description.length < 40) gaps.push(`${id}: description`)
        for (const p of (op.parameters ?? []) as Json[]) if (typeof p.description !== 'string') gaps.push(`${id}: parameter ${p.name as string}`)
        const responses = op.responses as Record<string, Json>
        const ok = Object.entries(responses).find(([code]) => code.startsWith('2'))
        const content = (ok?.[1].content as Record<string, Json> | undefined)?.['application/json']
        if (content !== undefined && content.example === undefined) gaps.push(`${id}: response example`)
        const body = ((op.requestBody as Json | undefined)?.content as Record<string, Json> | undefined)?.['application/json']
        if (body !== undefined && body.example === undefined) gaps.push(`${id}: request example`)
      }
    }
    for (const [name, schema] of Object.entries((doc.components as Json).schemas as Record<string, Json>)) {
      if (typeof schema.description !== 'string') gaps.push(`schema ${name}: description`)
      for (const [where, prop] of properties(schema, name)) {
        if (typeof prop.description !== 'string' && prop.$ref === undefined) gaps.push(`${where}: description`)
      }
    }
    for (const [code, entry] of Object.entries(ERROR_CODES)) {
      if (typeof (entry as { remedy?: unknown }).remedy !== 'string') gaps.push(`error ${code}: remedy`)
    }
    it('has no gap — every operation, parameter, schema, property and error code is described, and every operation has its examples', () => {
      expect(gaps.filter((g) => !ALLOWED_GAPS.includes(g))).toEqual([])
    })
    it('names nothing a reader outside the team cannot resolve', () => {
      const hits = JSON.stringify(doc).split('","').filter((s) => INTERNAL.test(s))
      expect(hits).toEqual([])
    })
    it('has every example PARSE through its own schema', () => {
      for (const route of ROUTE_DEFINITIONS) {
        expect(route.success.schema.safeParse(route.examples.response).success, `${route.operationId}'s response example`).toBe(true)
        if (route.examples.request !== undefined)
          expect(route.body.safeParse(route.examples.request).success, `${route.operationId}'s request example`).toBe(true)
      }
    })
  })
  ```
  **A `$ref` property takes its description from the component it names** — which is why the property check skips `$ref`s and the component check does not. Run it: **Expected: FAIL, with the gap list** — record its length at the start (Task 1's `[M6]` predicts ~600 lines: 556 properties, the operations, the parameters, the error codes' remedies). **The last `it` cannot pass until `examples` is required** — make it required in `route.ts` now, and `pnpm typecheck` lists every route that has none.

- [ ] **Step 2: Error codes — `remedy` on all of them.** `Entry` gains `remedy: string`: **what a caller does next**, in one or two sentences, addressed to a person or an agent alike — *"Read the tree again for its `commitSha`, recompute your changes against it, and commit with that as `baseCommit`."*, never *"An error occurred."* A code with no remedy a caller can act on says so and why: `INTERNAL`'s remedy is *"Retry once; if it recurs, the platform's operator has a line naming it — report the time and the operation."* `document.ts` publishes `x-enumDescriptions` on `ErrorCode` (`{ [code]: summary }`) and a top-level `x-manifest-errors` (`{ [code]: { status, summary, remedy } }`), both generated from `ERROR_CODES` and sorted. **`schema.d.ts` must not change for this step** — run `pnpm contract:generate` and read its diff; `x-` keys are extensions `openapi-typescript` ignores.

- [ ] **Step 3: Events.** Every schema in `EVENT_DETAIL_SCHEMAS` and each of its fields gets `.describe()`; `websocket.ts`'s `streamPathItem()` gains `x-manifest-event-types`: `EVENT_TYPES.map((type) => ({ type, description, example: EXAMPLE_DETAILS[type] }))`, where `description` is the schema's own. **Add to the gate** a check that every event type has a description of at least one sentence and an example that parses through its schema.

- [ ] **Step 4: `manifest.yaml`'s JSON Schema.** By Task 1's `[M7]` rule: `zodToJsonSchema(manifestSchema, { $refStrategy: 'none', target: 'jsonSchema2019-09' })` (or the target `[M7]` recorded as working), `$schema` stripped, published as `components.schemas.ManifestYaml` with a description saying it is **documentation for the file** — the platform validates with §7's own code, and a JSON Schema cannot express every rule (an `env` entry carries a value or `secret: true`, never both) — and `Spec.spec`'s description names it. **Read how Ajv in `@manifest/mock` compiles components** (`validate.ts`): a component it cannot compile breaks the mock for every operation; if it objects, record it and publish the schema as a served document in Task 11 instead of a component.

- [ ] **Step 5: The descriptions and the examples, module by module, until the gap list is empty.** Work in this order, running the gate after each and committing each module separately so a session limit loses nothing: `schemas.ts` (the shared types — `Uuid`, `Timestamp`, `ManifestError`), then representations **by tag**: projects, delivery, source, secrets, launch, releases, tokens, pending-actions, blueprints, fleet, identity, then every route's `description` (**a sentence a person outside the team can act on**: what it does, who may call it, what it answers, and the one thing most likely to surprise — *"A deploy answers once the new instance serves, not at once"*), then every route's `examples` — **a real answer**: run the route in its own test, `console.log(JSON.stringify(res.json(), null, 2))` once, shorten any list to one or two entries, and keep every field. **The rules for the prose**, which Task 11's guides follow too: plain English; say what a field IS and when it is null; name the operation to call next by its `operationId`; cite the spec's section (`§13`) for depth and never a plan, a task, a sitting, a finding or a person; **and correct every stale sentence the gate's second `it` finds** — *"D9. Reported, not yet enforced (P6)."* on `SensitiveDiff` is the first (it is enforced: at the production deploy, against the last approved release).

- [ ] **Step 6: An independent linter.** `api/contract/lint.test.ts` lints `packages/contract/openapi.json` with `@redocly/openapi-core`'s `recommended` rules (the API Task 1's `[M8]` read); the test asserts **no problem of severity `error`, and exactly the warnings in a named list**, each with its reason — e.g. `operation-4xx-response`: every operation's errors are the `default` response in the D23.7 envelope, by design. **An empty list is the goal; a rule is disabled only with a sentence.**

- [ ] **Step 7: The gates; commit.** `pnpm contract:write && pnpm contract:generate` (read the diff of `schema.d.ts`: descriptions become JSDoc comments, and **no type may change** — a changed type is a representation that moved, and that is a finding); the four gates; `pnpm test` twice. Commit per module as Step 5 says, and last:
  ```bash
  git commit -m "docs(api): the reference is complete — every operation, field, error code and event described, every operation with its example, held by a gate"
  ```

- [ ] **Step 8: Watch it fail.** (a) Delete one property's `.describe()` — predict the gate red naming exactly that property's path. (b) Change one example's field to the wrong type — predict *has every example PARSE* red naming the operation. (c) Put *"(the D5 plan's Decision 18)"* back into a description — predict the second `it` red. (d) **Remove the `description` check for PARAMETERS from the gate** — predict it stays green (every parameter is now described), and **say so**: a gate that is green because the work is done cannot show its own control; (a) is what shows it.

    { step: 'Answer an agent’s question', who: 'person', operations: ['confirmPendingAction', 'rejectPendingAction'] },
    { step: 'See what a first launch needs, rehearse, and record UBC’s answers', who: 'either', operations: ['getLaunchReadiness', 'getLaunchRecords', 'runRehearsal', 'recordIamRegistration', 'recordPrivacyAssessment'] },
    { step: 'Approve a release from a stored preview', who: 'administrator', operations: ['createApprovalPreview', 'getApprovalPreview', 'approveRelease', 'rejectRelease', 'getApproval'] },
    { step: 'Manage who works on the project', who: 'person', operations: ['listMembers', 'addMember', 'removeMember', 'listProjects', 'getProject'] },
    { step: 'Change who the app is for', who: 'person', operations: [], later: { plan: 'the admin console’s queue (§26)', why: '§24: raising an audience is a request an administrator approves' } },
    { step: 'Delete a project, or roll back a release', who: 'person', operations: [], later: { plan: 'not yet placed', why: 'named in P6b’s What this plan does not build' } },
    { step: 'Work on a branch in a sandbox, with exec', who: 'agent', operations: [], later: { plan: 'Phase 3', why: 'S5 — an agent inside a sandbox — is unrun (§17)' } },
  ]
  /** In the document, and deliberately in no faculty step — each with its reason. */
  export const OUTSIDE_THE_JOURNEY: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {
    listFleet: 'An administrator’s view of every app (§26), not a step in building one.',
  }
  ```
  `coverage.test.ts` reads `packages/contract/openapi.json` (by `node:fs`) and refuses: an operation a step names that the document lacks; **an operation the document has that no step names and `OUTSIDE_THE_JOURNEY` does not list**; a step with no operations and no `later`. **Every operation must appear** — if the list above misses one, the test names it and this step is not done until it is placed.

- [ ] **Step 2: The examples, as real files, run against the mock.** One file per example a guide shows, flat in `packages/journey/src/` (the journey's boundary allows `@manifest/contract`, `node:` builtins and siblings only — *Read this first*'s check of `boundary.test.ts`), each exporting one async function taking a client:
  ```ts
  // packages/journey/src/example-commit.ts — authoring.md's "commit a change" example
  import { createManifestClient, idempotencyKey, unwrap } from '@manifest/contract'

  export async function commitAChange(origin: string, token: string, projectId: string): Promise<string> {
    const client = createManifestClient({ origin, token })
    // 1. Read what you are changing — and keep the commit it was read at.
    const tree = unwrap(await client.GET('/v1/projects/{projectId}/tree', { params: { path: { projectId } } }), 'getTree')
    // 2. Commit against exactly that commit. A retry reuses the same key.
    const key = idempotencyKey()
    const outcome = unwrap(
      await client.POST('/v1/projects/{projectId}/commits', {
        params: { path: { projectId }, header: { 'Idempotency-Key': key } },
        body: {
          baseCommit: tree.commitSha,
          message: 'Add a replies count to each post',
          changes: [{ op: 'write', path: 'public/app.js', content: '/* … */\n' }],
        },
      }),
      'createCommit',
    )
    return outcome.commitSha!
  }
  ```
  **`createManifestClient({ origin, token })` is the client's own option** (`packages/contract/src/client.ts:22`) — read the file for the exact option names. `examples.test.ts` starts `createMockServer` from `@manifest/mock` on an ephemeral port and runs every `example-*.ts` against it, asserting each answers the shape the guide claims. `pnpm typecheck` compiles them. The guides' examples to write: sign in and read `getMe` (session); mint and use a token; read the tree and a file; commit a change; a dry run that is refused `SPEC_INVALID`, and reading `details`; handle `SOURCE_CONFLICT` by reading again; set a secret; start a build and watch it on the stream (`subscribe`); release and deploy; read the launch checklist; an agent waiting on a pending action.

- [ ] **Step 3: The guides, hand-written.** Each page is for **two readers at once — a front-end developer and an AI agent** — and follows Task 9's prose rules. Each page opens with one paragraph saying what it is for and who it is for, and every code block is an `<!-- example: <file> -->` marker that `docs:write` fills. What each page must cover:

  | Page | Must cover |
  |---|---|
  | `index.md` | What Manifest's API is, the two credential classes, where each page is, and the one rule an agent must never break: **a privileged action waits for a person** (D24) |
  | `getting-started.md` | From nothing to a deployed app, as the shortest correct path, pointing into the others |
  | `authentication.md` | Sessions (CWL, the cookie, `Origin`), delegated tokens (minting, scope to one project, capabilities, rate limits, revocation), step-up, and `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` for a Node client on the laptop |
  | `conventions.md` | `/v1` and versioning; `Idempotency-Key` (one per user action, reused on retry); the error envelope — switch on `code`, read `hint`, `details`; `202` and the stream; pagination (`cursor`/`next`); limits |
  | `journey.md` | The end-to-end path, as the table `docs:write` generates from `JOURNEY` — including every `later` step and its plan |
  | `authoring.md` | Tree, file, history, one commit; `baseCommit` and `SOURCE_CONFLICT`; writes and deletions; the dry run; what is refused and why (paths, text only, sizes, secrets, the manifest); who a commit is attributed to; builds use their own commit's manifest; **and that a `Dockerfile` or `.npmrc` an agent commits is replaced by the blueprint's at build (D13)** — accepted, and ignored, so an agent is told rather than surprised |
  | `secrets.md` | Declaring a secret in `manifest.yaml`, setting it per environment, why it is never read back, the refused deploy, production's step-up |
  | `events.md` | The stream: frames, replay, ready, close codes, reconnecting; and every event type (the generated table) |
  | `launching.md` | The checklist, the rehearsal, the approval and its stored preview, re-escalation on a sensitive change, what an agent may and may not do here |
  | `agents.md` | **For an AI agent**: the loop (read → change → check → commit → build → deploy → read the result), the rules (switch on codes; never guess a path — list the tree; always send `baseCommit`; reuse the `Idempotency-Key` on retry; a `TOKEN_ACTION_PENDING` means wait for a person, and how), what it may never do, and the knowledge pack's place (how to write an app for this blueprint) beside this guide (how to drive Manifest) |

- [ ] **Step 4: `docs:write`, and its gate.** `docs-write.ts` (a) fills every `<!-- example: <file> -->` … `<!-- /example -->` span with the file's text, fenced as `ts`; (b) writes `reference/operations.md` (every operation: method, path, tag, who may call it — derived from the matrix? **no**: from each operation's description, which says so — its parameters, request and response examples, and its error codes with their remedies), `reference/errors.md` (from `x-manifest-errors`), `reference/events.md` (from `x-manifest-event-types`) and `reference/manifest-yaml.md` (from `ManifestYaml`); (c) generates `journey.md`'s table between markers; (d) writes `docs/api/llms.txt` — a title, one paragraph, and a link line per page with its one-sentence summary — and copies it to `packages/console/public/llms.txt`. `docs.test.ts` asserts: **the committed files equal what `docs:write` would write** (it runs the generator into memory and compares); every backticked `operationId`, error code and event type a hand-written page names **exists** in the document; every relative link resolves to a file. Run `pnpm docs:write`, commit what it wrote.

- [ ] **Step 5: Served over the API (Decision 18).** `docs/load.ts`'s `loadApiDocs(root)` reads every `*.md` under `docs/api/` at boot — bounded (each page ≤ 512 KiB, ≤ 100 pages), UTF-8, each page's `title` from its first `# ` line and `summary` from its first paragraph — and **refuses the boot naming a page it cannot read**, as `loadBlueprints` refuses a broken blueprint. A page's `slug` is its path without `.md`, with `/` as `-` (`reference/errors.md` → `reference-errors`). The three routes: `listDocs` → `DocIndex { pages: [{ slug, title, summary }] }`; `getDoc` → `DocPage { slug, title, markdown }`, `404 DOC_NOT_FOUND` for a slug that is not a page; `getOpenApiDocument` → `openApiDocument(ROUTE_DEFINITIONS)` itself, computed once at boot (the same function `contract:write` runs, so the served document is the published one — **the test compares it with `packages/contract/openapi.json`, byte for byte after `JSON.stringify`**). **Any credential**: each route calls `requireActor` and no `assertCapability` — a token of any project reads them, because documentation is no project's data; anonymous is `401`. Three rows in the matrix: every actor `pass` but `anonymous` (401). `OpenApiDocument`'s representation is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`, described.

Record the screenshot and the log in the sitting's record.

- [ ] **Step 7: The knowledge pack points at the guide.** `AGENTS.md` gains *Putting your code into Manifest*: two paragraphs — the app is committed through `createCommit` against the `commitSha` you read, `manifest.yaml` is validated before anything is written, and `GET /v1/docs/agents` is how to drive the platform — and nothing that restates the guide. **The blueprint's own tests read the knowledge pack** (bounded, at load): run them.

  ```bash
  git commit -m "docs(api): the guides, the generated reference and the journey — served at /v1/docs and /v1/openapi.json, in the console, and as an HTML reference"

(b) Edit `reference/errors.md` by hand — predict the drift case red. (c) Delete `getTree` from `JOURNEY` — predict `coverage.test.ts` red naming it. (d) Make an example call an operation with a wrong body field — predict `pnpm typecheck` red (the generated client's types), **and the mock run green or red?** — predict, then record: the mock validates RESPONSES, not requests, so the run may stay green, which is why `typecheck` is the gate for examples' requests.

- Produces: `scanNewCommits(gitDir, heads, exclude, limits?)` answering `{ findings, commits, unscannable: string[] }` — **no `truncated`**; the event `repository.scan_incomplete { commits: string[] }`; `RepositoryLink.visibility: 'private' | 'public' | null`; `require_driver`; **`RouteDefinition.withholdOnReplay`** and the code **`409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED`** (Step 5).

- [ ] **Step 1: The scan pages to the end (the D5 plan's final review, Important 3, ruled for this plan).** Today one scan reads at most 1000 commits and 20 MiB of patch, then marks the branch scanned — so what lay past the cap is never scanned. **Page instead**: `git rev-list --reverse --topo-order --stdin` lists every new commit oldest first; scan them in batches of `SCAN_COMMIT_LIMIT` with `git log -p --no-walk --stdin` over each batch's ids (`PATCH_ARGS` unchanged); a batch whose patch exceeds `SCAN_OUTPUT_LIMIT` is split in half and retried; **a single commit whose own patch exceeds the limit is `unscannable`** — reported, never silently marked. The driver publishes `repository.scan_incomplete` naming those commits **before** it moves `refs/manifest/scanned/*`, and a build of such a commit is still refused by the build gate if it holds a secret (the gate scans every tree). Test with `limits: { commits: 3, bytes: 4096 }` passed in: 10 commits → 4 batches, every finding found; one 8 KiB commit → `unscannable`, the rest scanned. **Red first**: the 10-commit case finds only the first batch's finding against today's code.

- [ ] **Step 2: No PUID in any event's sentence.** `launch/records.ts:246` and `:314` name the person as `releases/approval.ts:86-90` does. **Then sweep**: `grep -rn "actor.puid\|\.puid}" src/ --include=*.ts | grep -v test` — every `humanMessage` that interpolates a PUID is changed, and each is listed in the commit message. A test per changed publisher: the sentence contains the display name and not the PUID.

- [ ] **Step 3: The six minors, each red first.**
  1. **A bodyless POST with a well-formed signature answers `500`** (`api/routes/webhooks.ts`, the HMAC over `undefined`) → `400 WEBHOOK_PAYLOAD_INVALID`, beside an accepted delivery.
  2. **`localGitDir` fails OPEN on a mirror with no `manifest.visibility`** → a mirror whose visibility was never read is synced first; if the sync cannot read it (offline), `503 SOURCE_UNREACHABLE` — **fail closed**.
  3. **The console's Code line says *private* whatever GitHub last said** → `RepositoryLink.visibility` is the mirror's last read (`null` on driver 1), and the line says what it says.
  4. **No production guard against the fake's `MANIFEST_GITHUB_*` defaults** → `config.ts` refuses `MANIFEST_GITHUB_API_URL` naming `127.0.0.1:7110` (the fake) outside development, as `CONFIG_BUILD_CREDENTIAL_SECRET_REQUIRED` refuses its default — **read how `config.ts` tells development from not**, and reuse it.
  5. **`gitWithToken` inherits `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`** → its environment is built from `PATH` and its own keys, as Task 3's `runGit` is — **and the canary test stays green**.
  6. **`SummaryContext` carries `review` and `coverage`, which the summary no longer reads** → removed from the interface; the snapshot keeps both fields.

- [ ] **Step 4: Every driver-1 demo refuses a driver-2 control plane before it creates anything** (the D5 plan's *Added at the close*: *"a driver-1 demo run on a driver-2 control plane creates its project there, where no route deletes it"*). `require_driver local` in `scripts/lib/api.sh`: an unsigned `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' --data '{}' http://127.0.0.1:$PORT_CONTROL_PLANE/webhooks/github` — `404` is driver 1, `401` driver 2 — and on the wrong one it prints the restart command and exits 1. Called at the top of `demo.sh`, `demo-identity.sh`, `demo-ai.sh`, `demo-redeploy.sh`, `demo-journey.sh`, `demo-token.sh`, `demo-production.sh` and `demo-releases.sh` — **`grep -ln 'POST /v1/projects\|create_project' scripts/demo*.sh` lists which create projects; every one of them**.

- [ ] **Step 5: A delegated token's secret is kept in NO idempotency record — sitting 5's F4, Rich's option (a) (decided 2026-09-26; *Decided by Rich*).** **What is true today, measured by sitting 5:** `replayOrStore` (`api/idempotency.ts`) stores whatever a mutation answered, so `mintToken`'s record in `idempotency_keys.response_body` holds `MintedToken` — `{ token, secret }` — with the plaintext secret, and nothing ever deletes a row; `mintToken`'s own description says *"the platform stores only a hash of it and cannot show it again"* (D24). It is the ONLY route whose answer carries a credential: the registry token (`api/routes/registry-token.ts`) is a plain `app.post` outside the idempotency wrapper — **re-check that with `grep -rn "secret\|password\|token" api/representations/*.ts` before you rely on it.** **What to build:**
  1. **`RouteDefinition` gains `withholdOnReplay?: { stored: (answer) => unknown; refuse: (stored: unknown) => Error }`** — a route whose answer carries a credential is NEVER replayed. The wrapper hands it to `app.idempotent` (`api/server.ts`'s decorator), which hands it to `replayOrStore`: the record keeps `stored(answer)`, and a repeated key **with the same fingerprint** throws `refuse(stored)`. **The fingerprint check stays FIRST** — the same key with a different body is still `409 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` — and a refusal stores nothing, as today (the catch's placement in `route.ts` is load-bearing; read its comment).
  2. **`mintToken` declares it**: `stored: ({ token }) => ({ token })` — the `Token` representation, exactly what `listTokens` shows, never the secret — and `refuse` answers **`TokenAlreadyMintedError`**, which `replayOrStore` throws (`api/errors.ts`, `readonly code = 'TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED'`, the shape `IdempotencyConflictError` has, so `error-codes.test.ts`'s scan finds it), mapped by `instanceof` to **`409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED`**. Its message names the token the first request minted — its id and name — and its hint says what to do: *"The first request minted this token; its secret was shown once and is not kept. If that answer was lost, revoke it (`revokeToken`) and mint again with a new Idempotency-Key."* A row written BEFORE this step holds `{ token, secret }`; `refuse` reads `token` from it and never replays the rest.
  3. **Register the code** in `api/error-codes.ts` (`api(409, …)`) **with its `remedy`** — Task 9 made `remedy` required on every code — and add it to `mintToken`'s `errors`. **Change `mintToken`'s description** so a reader learns that a retry with the same key answers the refusal rather than the secret. `pnpm contract:write && pnpm contract:generate` **inside `1.3.0`** (never bump).
  4. **Scrub what is already stored, in a CUSTOM migration** — no schema changes, so `db:generate` writes nothing: `pnpm --filter @manifest/control-plane exec drizzle-kit generate --custom --name scrub_minted_token_secrets` writes an empty numbered file and its journal entry (checked on drizzle-kit 0.31.10: `generate --help` lists `--custom`); fill it, commented as 0024's backfill is:
     ```sql

     -- before this migration. Idempotent.
     UPDATE "idempotency_keys" SET "response_body" = "response_body" - 'secret'
       WHERE "route" = 'POST /v1/projects/:projectId/tokens' AND "response_body" ? 'secret';
     ```
     **Measure it on a real row before trusting it**: with the control plane running on the tree BEFORE this step, mint a token through the edge; apply the migration (`db:migrate`, ORIENTATION §3's one-line form); `select count(*) from idempotency_keys where response_body ? 'secret'` answers `0`, and **the token still authenticates** (its hash in `delegated_tokens` is untouched).
  5. **`manifest-mock` does the same** (`packages/mock/src/server.ts`'s `seen` store replays by key): for `mintToken` it keeps the answer without `secret` and answers a replay `409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED` — a front end built against the mock meets what the platform does. A case beside the mock's other replay cases.
  6. **The guides**: Task 11 wrote `docs/api/` against the old behaviour. Change every page that says what a replayed mutation answers (`conventions.md`, `authentication.md` — `grep -rln "Idempotency-Key\|replay" docs/api/`) and run `pnpm docs:write`; its drift gate goes red until you do.

  **The tests, red first** (`api/tokens.test.ts`): **a mint retried with the same Idempotency-Key is `409 TOKEN_ALREADY_MINTED`, naming the token — and exactly ONE token exists, and NO idempotency record holds its secret** (the first answer DID carry it: that is the positive control, and the record's `token.id` is the second); the same key with a different body is still `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; **another route still replays** (`createProject` twice with one key → `201` and the same id). Red against today's code at the replay (`201` with the secret) and at the record. `api/idempotency.test.ts`: `withholdOnReplay` stores `stored(answer)`, refuses a replay, and checks the fingerprint first. **The console needs no change** — `screens/tokens.tsx` mints with `api.newKey()` per submit — and no demo replays a mint (`grep -rn "tokens'" packages/journey/src/`), checked by sitting 5. **A retention for `idempotency_keys` is NOT built** (*What this plan does not build*). Commit:

## Task 13: The acceptance — `make demo-authoring`: an agent builds the bulletin board from the bare skeleton through the API, on both drivers, and a person uses it

> **`[S4]` — WHAT TASKS 6 AND 7 BUILT THAT THIS TASK DRIVES (2026-09-26, sitting 4; its record).** `createCommit` answers
> `201` with `CommitOutcome` (`changes` sorted by path); an agent's token needs **`source:write`** (and `project:read` to
> read what it writes) — `project:write` alone is `403 FORBIDDEN`. **A build of a named commit freezes THAT commit's
> manifest**, validating it first if nobody has; the empty body builds the newest recorded validation's commit. Driven
> through the edge on driver 1 in sitting 4: a 7 MiB commit body passes the edge (`201`) and a 9 MiB one is `413` from the
> control plane, so the edge adds no smaller limit. **Not measured, and worth knowing on driver 2:** driver 2's commit
> syncs its mirror after the push, and the mirror's observer publishes `repository.pushed` for any branch that moved —
> so an API commit there probably reads as BOTH `repository.committed` and `repository.pushed`, and the fake's webhook
> delivery for it then finds the head already validated. Assert what is seen, not this.

> **`[S5]` — WHAT TASK 8 BUILT THAT THIS TASK DRIVES (2026-09-26, sitting 5; its record).** `setAppSecret` refuses a
> value under **six characters** (`400 REQUEST_INVALID`), so the board's `BOARD_ADMIN_CODE` is at least that. Step 8's
> refusal is built (`409 RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET`, the name in the message, no instance row) and step 10's
> (`403 TOKEN_CREDENTIAL_REFUSED`). **Control (d)'s prediction below is WRONG as written**: with the
> `RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET` check removed, the deploy is NOT healthy — `renderInjection` now refuses a declared secret with
> no value (`INJECTION_SECRET_MISSING`), inside the deploy's `try`, so the answer is **`500 INTERNAL` with an instance row
> left in `starting`** (measured in sitting 5, control (b)). Predict that, or remove both checks to see the old healthy
> deploy. `app_secret.set` names the agent's token in its sentence — step 9's *"the value appears in no event"* holds (the
> event's redactor is built with the value, too).

**ALONE, AND LAST.** It drives the authoring path end to end through nothing but `@manifest/contract` — **as an agent, holding a delegated token** — from an empty skeleton to a running bulletin board, on driver 1 and on driver 2. It is **green three times on each driver**, a step of the offline acceptance and of `make ci-acceptance`, and **clicked by a person**: a student posts a question and an instructor replies.

> **`[S6]` (2026-09-26, sitting 6):** nothing this task drives changed shape. Every `RouteDefinition` must carry `examples`
> (`tsc` refuses one without), and the document is gated, so the acceptance can rely on every operation `/v1/openapi.json`
> serves (Task 11) having a description, an example and its error codes' remedies.

**Files:**
- Create: `fixtures/bulletin-board/manifest.yaml`, `fixtures/bulletin-board/server.js`, `fixtures/bulletin-board/public/index.html`, `fixtures/bulletin-board/public/app.js` — the app the agent writes
- Create: `packages/journey/src/authoring.ts` — the phases, through `@manifest/contract` and nothing else; `scripts/demo-authoring.sh` — sign-ins (`idp_login`), minting the agent's token, a PERSON's git push of a symlink, and the driver check
- Modify: `Makefile` — `demo-authoring: up`; `scripts/offline-acceptance.sh` — step 14; `scripts/ci-acceptance.sh` — the step
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/RUNBOOK.md` — *`make demo-authoring`*, on each driver; `docs/superpowers/WALKTHROUGH.md` — what to open, and the two screens

**The app.** `server.js` builds on the skeleton exactly as the proof app does — `configureCwl`, `sessionMiddleware`, the Mongo client from `MONGODB_URI`/`MONGODB_DB_NAME` — and serves: `GET /` (the page), `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/posts` (questions, newest first, each with its replies and its author's name), `POST /api/posts` `{ title, body }`, `POST /api/posts/:id/replies` `{ body }`, `POST /api/posts/:id/pin` (allowed only with the header `X-Board-Admin-Code` equal to `BOARD_ADMIN_CODE` — **the secret, compared in constant time**), `GET /api/status` → `{ adminCodeConfigured: boolean }` (whether the variable is set — **never its value**), and `/healthz`. `manifest.yaml` declares `services: [{ type: mongo, version: "7", name: db }]`, `auth: { provider: cwl, attributes: [ubcEduCwlPuid, givenName, sn, eduPersonAffiliation] }` with each attribute's reason in a comment (as the proof app's does), `env: [{ name: BOARD_ADMIN_CODE, secret: true }]`, `data: { classification: internal, retention_days: 120 }`, and **no AI**. The page is plain HTML: the list, a form, a reply box.

- [ ] **Step 1: The phases, and what each asserts — the shape of every answer, and every refusal's code.**

| Step | What happens | Asserted |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Which driver: an unsigned `POST /webhooks/github` | `404 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` → driver 1, slug `board-local`; `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING` → driver 2, slug `board-github`. Anything else: stop, naming it, **creating nothing** |
| 1 | `instructor` signs in (bash, `idp_login`), creates the project **with no starter** or re-uses it, and **mints the agent's token**: `project:read`, `source:write`, `secret:write`, `build:create`, `release:create`, `release:deploy` | the project's `repository.provider` matches step 0's driver; the token's capabilities exactly those six |
| 2 | **The agent reads the documentation first**: `GET /v1/docs/agents`, the knowledge pack, and `getTree` at `main` | the guide names `createCommit` and `baseCommit`; the tree holds the skeleton's `server.js` and `manifest.yaml`; `commitSha` recorded as the base |
| 3 | A dry run of the board with a mistyped manifest (`classification: secret`) | `422 SPEC_INVALID`, `details[0].path` `data.classification`; `main` unmoved |
| 4 | The board committed: four writes, and a `NOTES.md` | `201`, `changes` exactly those five paths `added`/`modified`; `spec.sensitiveDiff.fields` includes `auth.attributes` and `services`; on the stream, `repository.committed` whose sentence names **the instructor's agent and the token's name**, and `spec.validated` for that commit |
| 5 | The refusals, each beside step 4's accepted commit: the step-2 base again; a file with an AWS-key-shaped value (built at run time); `.git/config`; a file named `public` (a directory); a delete of `nope.txt`; the same content again | `409 SOURCE_CONFLICT`; `409 SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED` **and a `repository.secret_refused` event whose JSON holds no value**; `400 REQUEST_INVALID`; `409 SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT`; `409 SOURCE_PATH_NOT_FOUND`; `409 SOURCE_NOTHING_TO_COMMIT` — **`main` still step 4's commit after all six** |
| 6 | **The symlink attack**: a PERSON pushes `link → .git` (driver 1: into the bare repository; driver 2: to the fake), then the agent commits `link/config` holding a `core.fsmonitor` that would touch a marker | `409 SOURCE_PATH_CONFLICT`; **the marker file does not exist**; then the agent deletes `NOTES.md` against the new head — `201`, `deleted` |
| 7 | History | `listCommits`: the agent's commits `madeThrough: { kind: 'agent', name: <instructor>, tokenName }`; the person's symlink push `madeThrough: null`; `getCommit` of step 4 has a patch for `server.js` |
| 8 | Build **step 4's commit — not the newest** — then release, then deploy to staging **before the secret is set** | the build `succeeded`, its spec's commit is step 4's (Task 7); the deploy `409 RELEASE_SECRET_NOT_SET` naming `BOARD_ADMIN_CODE`, and no new instance |
| 9 | The agent sets the staging secret; deploys again | `setAppSecret` `200` `set: true` (no value in the body); the deploy `healthy`; `GET /` on the app's URL answers with `X-Manifest-Instance` = the new instance **and a body containing `Bulletin board`** (the shape — the edge's wildcard answers `200` for anything); `GET /api/status` → `{ adminCodeConfigured: true }`; **the value appears in no event, no build log and no answer** — grep every frame and body the run collected |
| 10 | The agent tries production's secret | `403 TOKEN_CREDENTIAL_REFUSED` (Spec action 2's answer; if Rich declined it, `200` and the row says so) |
| 11 | People use it: `student` signs into the app (`idp_login` against the app's hostname), posts a question; `instructor` replies | `GET /api/posts` holds the question by the student's name and the reply by the instructor's |

**The re-use path** (`board-*` exists) re-reads the tree for its base, and every step makes a new commit, so no step depends on the last run. **Step 5's secret is built at run time** from `SAMPLE_SECRETS`' recipe, never a real key.
